The exit ledger tells you the losses are entries, not exits. Of the 15 losers, 8 were flat-clock (−$160) and 7 were force-kill (−$125). But look at how far they ever went green: 13 of those 15 losers had a max-favorable excursion of essentially zero (the best was +0.08%). They never printed a profit to give back — they went red the second they were on and stayed red. That is the fingerprint of a bad entry, not a leaky exit. No amount of flat-clock tuning fixes a trade that is never green.

§9.2 2 · Fertile conditions — where MES actually makes money

I swept every entry condition in the funnel. One knob separates the winners from the losers almost by itself: bar size (atr_pct — the height of a 5-second bar as a % of price, i.e. how violent the tape is). Calm tape pays; violent tape robs.

Condition sweptnNet $Win%Plain meaning

atr_pct 0.02–0.0512−28.7558Getting choppy — win often, lose big

SHORT side20−55.0045Most fades-of-strength were shorts

The money cliff — net P&L by bar-size (atr_pct)

It is close to monotone: the busier the bars, the more MES loses. The mechanism is real and desk-wide — calm tape mean-reverts (a pullback is just noise the trend absorbs), a vol-expansion breaks and keeps going (the "pullback" you sold is actually the start of a reversal that runs you over). Note the trap in the middle band: 58% and 67% win rates that still lose money, because the few losers are 5× the size of the wins. Win rate lies here; the money doesn't.

FERTILE POCKET — PROVEN

MES is fertile when the bars are small (atr_pct ≤ 0.03) AND it is NOT the US-open hour (outside 12:00–16:00 UTC). That pocket made +$138.75 over 17 trades at a 65% win rate. The complement — everything violent or midday — is 16 trades for −$153.75. The whole week's edge, and then some, lives in the calm pocket.

The footprint: this is quiet-drift absorption. In thin overnight/EU tape a gentle trend grinds one way, MES pulls back a few ticks against it, the pullback finds no follow-through (rvol ≤ 0.1 = nobody selling into it), and price resumes. The two fattest wins of the week are pure examples:

  • Thu 07-02, 04:53 UTC SHORT, +$73.75 — atr_pct 0.019, rvol 0.046 (dead), a clean overnight down-drift; held 3h09m to a SIGNAL_SELL exit.
  • Wed 07-01, 04:29 UTC SHORT, +$52.50 — atr_pct 0.014, rvol 0.016 (barely a tick trading), held 2h57m to MAX_HOLD.

§9.3 3 · Barren conditions — the one pocket to switch off

The mirror image is stark and small enough to name exactly. Three overlapping conditions — midday, big bars, busy volume — are all really the same regime (the US cash open blowing volatility into the tape). Here is what each removal buys you:

Switch-off ruleTrades cut$ removedTheir win%Note
atr_pct > 0.03 (any busy bars)11−143.7555Single cleanest filter — flips week to +$128.75
12:00–16:00 UTC (US open)9−106.2544The clock-based version of the same regime
rvol > 0.35 (crowd tape)7–10−61.2560Volume confirmation of the same thing
Combined: midday OR atr_pct>0.05 OR rvol>0.3512−108.7550Leaves 21 trades at +$93.75, 57% win
SWITCH OFF

Block MES vwap_pullback entries when atr_pct > 0.03 (or, equivalently, during 12:00–16:00 UTC). On this week that removes −$143.75 of losses across 11 trades and turns the MES book from −$15 to +$128.75. This is not merely "trade less" — the removed pocket has a genuinely negative expectancy (55% win rate but a −$13 average because the losers are catastrophic), and it has a mechanism (vol-expansion fades get run over). The calm pocket you keep is independently positive. Honesty note: n is small (33 total), so treat the −$143.75 as directional, not a promise — but the sign has agreed across atr_pct, rvol and clock, three independent cuts, which is what gives it weight.

The live gate almost does this already — and it is set too loose. MES carries an atr_pct ceiling of 0.09 on the pullback gate. But the single worst trade of the week, Monday's −$63.75 short at 14:27, entered at atr_pct 0.0986 — it should have been at or over the ceiling and blocked, yet it fired. And the money cliff shows the real danger line is not 0.09 but roughly 0.03. Recommendation: TUNE drop the MES atr_pct ceiling from 0.09 to ~0.03, and audit why the 0.0986 entry slipped the current 0.09 guard (likely the ceiling reads a rolling atr_pct that differs from the entry snapshot).

Isn't "only trade calm tape" just cowardice / trading less?
No — the kept pocket (calm, off-midday) is independently profitable at +$138.75 / 65% win, and the removed pocket is independently negative with a real mechanism (fading a vol-expansion). If it were pure "trade less" the kept trades would be near zero; instead they carry the whole edge. We are removing a loss engine, not shrinking a coin-flip.

Midday US-open is where MES's biggest moves live and where its worst losses live — because it keeps fading the very moves it should be riding. Step one (bankable now): stop fading it (P1/P2), which alone flips the week green. Step two (the upside, unproven): build a momentum-with entry to actually harvest the $150–$258/contract breakouts we currently watch go by.

§10 MGC Dossier — Micro Gold, the Contract That Wants Strength

MGC lost −$491 over 49 trades this week (55% win) — but the money map inside that loss is loud and unusually clean. Gold does the opposite of the desk story. Our other micros bled fading strong moves; MGC's strong-move trades were its only profit pocket (+$137 at 75% win). The bleed all sits in a narrow "half-finished move" band and in three long-hold orphans. And the three biggest 15-minute moves of the week — worth ~$1,700 of travel — happened in dead-quiet air-pockets our gold gate structurally cannot touch.

MGC's problem is trade selection, not trade management. When a gold trade works, the ratchet banks it (+$565). The loss is bad entries that never go green and then die on force-kill or the clock. So the fix is a filter on when to fire, not a new exit.

§10.4 2  Fertile conditions — gold rewards the FULLY-spent move

Here is the money finding, and it inverts the desk narrative. On MNQ/MES this week, fading a strong move lost because the move kept going. On gold it is the reverse: the harder the impulse before the pullback, the better the fade worked — because a 2+ ATR gold spike is exhausted and snaps back. It's the half-hearted move that keeps running and kills you.

Below, "move-strength" is net_atr — how many ATRs of net travel the impulse had before we entered the pullback. Read it as: how spent is the move.

Move strength (|net_atr|)Plain meaningnNet $Win%Avg $
0.0 – 0.5barely moved, noise7−11443−16.3
0.5 – 1.0half a move, still has legs9−24933−27.7
1.0 – 2.0a real move, maturing16−13056−8.1
2.0+fully spent spike → snap-back12+13775+11.4

The trend gauge tells the same story from the other side. When the VWAP slope was strongly up (slope ≥ 1) — an established, orderly trend that we bought pullbacks into — the book made money; the choppy in-between slopes bled.

Trend (vwap_slope_atr)Plain meaningnNet $Win%
−1 – −0.3soft, sloppy downtrend5−16120
−0.3 – +0.3flat / directionless4−2350
≥ +1.0clean, strong uptrend8+9062

Volume (rvol) is the trap: MGC's death zone is rvol 0.10–0.20 — a little bit of participation but not real conviction — which lost −$221 at 40% win over 10 trades. Dead-quiet (rvol < 0.1) was near-breakeven (−$58/20). Bar-size (atr_pct) barely discriminated on gold, and note MGC carries no atr_pct ceiling (unlike MNQ/MES at 0.09) — but that ceiling isn't the leak here; only 1 trade was above it.

FERTILE RULE
FERTILE when the impulse is fully spent (|net_atr| ≥ 2.0) OR the trend is clean and strong (slope ≥ 1.0). Combined pocket: 15 trades, +$143, 73% win. The footprint is a stop-run / exhaustion snap-back — gold overshoots on a flush, runs out of buyers/sellers, and reverts hard into our pullback fade. Everything else — the "maturing but unfinished" move — netted −$634 at 47% over 34 trades.

§10.5 3  Barren conditions — the half-finished move, and the orphans

The single worst structural pocket is the "half a move" band: |net_atr| between 0.5 and 2.0 — a move that has started but isn't spent. It netted −$379 over 25 trades at 48% win. This is exactly "fading a move that keeps going": we sell a gold pullback while the trend still has fuel, and it rolls right over us. Narrow it further to |net_atr| 0.5–2.0 with the low-conviction rvol 0.1–0.2 and you're at −$217 over 17.

Proof / sim: the half-move block removes ~−$380; the clock fix removes the three orphan holds (−$288). These overlap partly, but the floor of the two is well over −$400 of avoided loss on a −$491 week.

§10.10 Play D — NON-OBVIOUS: a coiled-spring breakout LONG for gold

SHADOW Gold's biggest edge this week wasn't a fade at all — it was the air-pocket breakout, and our fade-only gate cannot catch it (see §5). Build a shadow momentum-LONG entry keyed on the coil: 10-minute range < ~10 points AND collapsing volume, then enter on the first thrust bar that breaks the coil high.

Proof / sim: the two dead-quiet coils this week (Thu 02 Jul 12:28 and Tue 30 Jun 06:20) each broke into +$756 and +$475 15-minute runs. Even a modest 20-point capture on each ($200 × 2) with the ratchet trailing would have out-earned the entire live gold book. Shadow first — MGC has no live catch for this shape today.

§11 M2K Dossier — the small-caps micro we never traded, and the two-regime edge hiding in it

M2K (Micro Russell 2000, $5 a point) is observe-only — it took zero live trades this week. So this dossier is a promotion trial. I rebuilt what the live gates (vwap_pullback + vwap_dip) would have done by replaying all 112 signals they fired on M2K in the funnel against the real 5-second tape, exit-modelled exactly like the live desk (1-ATR disaster stop + ratchet give-back + 20-min flat-clock, minus a $1.20 spread/fee haircut per trade). The verdict on the current config is blunt: −$179 over 112 trades, 37% win — do NOT promote M2K as it is wired. But inside that loss are two clean, opposite edges: the fade works in dead-calm tape and gets run over in the US-open burst window, where we should be doing the exact opposite and riding it. Re-wired, the same 112 signals turn +$69 green.

Every dollar below is a simulation, not a booked trade — M2K is not live. Treat the direction and the shape of the edge as the signal; treat any single pocket's exact dollar as soft, and mind the thin n where flagged. One week, one contract.

M2K's price lived between 2988 and 3068 all week — a tight small-caps grind with one violent session each day around the US cash open. The gates fired 112 times, split almost perfectly 56 long / 56 short. Every entry family lost money except a 4-trade sliver of vwap_dip.

§11.3 The observe-only shadow agrees — even the ceiling is red

§11.4 2 · Fertile conditions — where M2K's fade actually pays

I swept every entry feature the gate records. The pattern is consistent and it has a mechanism: the reversion fade only works when the tape is genuinely dead. Sort by relative volume (rvol — how busy the tape is versus normal) and the edge appears at the quiet end.

Relative volume (rvol)TradesNet $Avg $Win%
< 0.15  (dead / nobody home)36−11.6−0.3250
0.15 – 0.30  (waking up)33−87.1−2.6427
0.30 – 0.60  (busy)43−80.5−1.8733

Quiet tape (rvol < 0.15) is near break-even at 50% — and it sharpens hard when you add a second filter: the tape must not be one-sided red. Requiring green_density ≥ 50 (at least half the recent bars closing up, i.e. two-sided balance, not a one-way flush) lifts it clean into the black:

FERTILE: rvol < 0.15 AND green_density ≥ 50 → +$34.0 over 18 trades, 61% win, +$1.89 each. This is absorption in a quiet book — the price wobbles a few ticks off VWAP with no volume behind it and gets pulled back. The fade is buying/selling a wobble nobody is committed to. The short side of this pocket is the sweetest: quiet-tape SHORTS were +$23.5 over 9, 67% win (thin n, flag it, but consistent).

By hour-of-day (UTC) the same edge shows as a session shape: London open (07–08h) and the US afternoon (15–19h) are green; the middle of the day is a furnace. Bars are sim net per session block.

§11.5 3 · Barren conditions — the switch-off list

Two filters remove almost all of M2K's pain, and they overlap on the same event: the US cash open.

Switch OFF when…TradesNet $Win%footprint
hour 09–14h UTC (US open / EU midday)39−148.121fading the open-drive burst
atr_pct < 0.025 (bars pinched flat)47−87.917dead-flat chop, whipsaw both ways
|vwap_slope| 0.5–1.5 (mild lean)57−114.832fading a slow one-way lean
THE ONE SWITCH

Killing the 09–14h UTC window removes −$148 of losses — 83% of the entire −$179 book — for a 21%-win pocket of 39 trades. These are the fades that get bulldozed: at 11:15 Mon a LONG fired with net_atr −3.50 (a hard down-drive) and green_density 30 — buying a knife, stopped for −$5.3. At 12:51 Mon a SHORT into net_atr +2.86 — selling a rip, stopped −$7.3. The gate has no idea the open-drive has started. But — see play 3 — switching off is leaving money on the table, because that same window is where M2K's biggest moves live.

§11.6 4 · The money — three plays, simulated

TUNE Play 1 — Fade only the dead tape. Arm M2K's pullback gate only when rvol < 0.15 AND green_density ≥ 50. That is the absorption-in-a-quiet-book pocket. Result: +$34 over 18 trades, 61% win, versus −$179 firing everywhere. Same gate, same ratchet — just a volume gate and a balance gate on the front door.

KILL Play 2 — Blackout the open drive. No M2K pullback entries 09:00–14:00 UTC. This is not a lazy time-tweak — it is the vol-expansion window where a mean-reversion gate is structurally wrong, and the data is unambiguous (21% win, −$148). Removes −$148 of losses.

RIDE Play 3 (non-obvious) — Flip the signal in the burst. This is the real prize. In the exact window and conditions where the fade dies, reverse it and ride. When bars are big (atr_pct ≥ 0.035 — the tape is expanding) during 09–14h, take the signal's opposite direction — momentum, not reversion. I re-ran those same signals flipped:

Flip-to-momentum pocketTradesNet $Avg $Win%
reverse, midday 09–14h AND atr_pct ≥ 0.03519+77.0+4.0568
reverse, any hour, atr_pct ≥ 0.05 (big bars)26+47.5+1.8362
note: reverse when |net_atr| ≥ 1.544−26.7−0.6139

The discriminator is atr_pct (whole-tape bar size / volatility expansion), not net_atr (this one signal's strength) — reversing on high net_atr alone loses. Ride when the market's bars are big, not when a single reading is big. Put the three together into one policy — fade the dead tape, ride the big-bar bursts, sit out everything in between — and the same 112 signals produce:

RE-WIRED M2K POLICY: fade if (rvol < 0.15 AND gd ≥ 50); else ride-the-flip if atr_pct ≥ 0.05; else stand aside.
→ +$68.8 over 40 trades, 60% win, +$1.72 each. A +$248 swing off the −$179 as-wired book — from trading a third as often, in only the two pockets that have a mechanism.

§11.7 5 · The missed money — M2K's biggest moves, and we weren't there

I scanned the 5-second tape for the largest 15-minute displacements of the week (close-to-close, times $5). Every one of the top six landed in the 13:00–14:15 UTC band — the very window play 2 blacks out for fading and play 3 flips to ride. And with one exception, the pullback gate never fired on them: a clean impulse has no pullback to fade, so the gate sits mute through the day's real money.

Thursday 14:07 is the whole M2K problem in miniature: the gate fired a LONG six minutes before price flushed −23 points (−$115). It faded a bull trap into an air-pocket and would have been long the whole way down. The fix is not "fade better" — it is to recognise the 13–14h regime and either stand aside or, per play 3, be the one riding the flush. Six moves worth ~$690 of range passed through M2K's most-active hour and the reversion gate caught none of them the right way.

§11.8 Verdict — conditional promotion, shadow-armed

Does M2K deserve to go live?
Not as wired — the pullback gate loses −$179 sim / −$105 shadow, and even the perfect-fill ceiling is red. But it is not a dead contract: it holds two real, opposite, mechanism-backed edges. Promote the re-wired policy (fade dead tape rvol<0.15 & gd≥50; flip-to-ride when atr_pct≥0.05; blackout everything else), which turns the same week +$69. Arm it in the shadow first for a fortnight — the pocket n's are 18–26, directionally clean but thin, and this is one week of a range-bound small-cap tape. If the shadow holds, M2K becomes the desk's first two-regime contract: reversion in the calm, momentum in the burst.

§12 MYM Dossier — the Micro-Dow: a quiet-tape fader pointed the wrong way at the open

MYM was observe-only all week — it never placed a live trade (the desk traded only MNQ, MES, MGC). So this dossier is built two ways: (1) a full-week shadow that replays the live vwap-pullback gate over MYM’s 60,389 scan cycles and prices every fired signal through the real 5-second tape (76k bars), and (2) the desk’s own realistic-fill shadow (shadow_real.db), which only has Fri 03-Jul but tells us the brutal truth about passive fills. The headline: MYM’s pullback gate is a coin-flip that nets roughly zero, its only real edge is reverting quiet tape, and the actual money on MYM this week — four $120–$141 bursts — happened at the New York open, where our gate was either asleep or fading the wrong way.

HOW TO READ THIS
MYM is the Micro E-mini Dow. Multiplier here is $0.50 per index point — the smallest tick-value on the desk. A 100-point Dow wiggle = $50 on one contract. The Dow ground up +734 points this week (52,425→53,159, range 52,322–53,400), so buy-and-hold beat every fade we ran. All P&L below is per one contract, net of ~$1/round-trip (spread + fee), unless stated.

§12.1 1. Scorecard — the shadow week

Replaying the live vwap_pullback gate over MYM’s week: 98 signals fired (49 long, 49 short, plus 1 lone vwap_dip). Priced through the tape with the live stop + 25%-give-back ratchet + 20-min flat-clock, the idealised book (assuming you always get filled at the signal price) looks like this:

Daily net — idealised shadow (per contract)

Two green days (Tue/Thu), three red. Wednesday 01-Jul was the worst (−$33 on 21 signals, 52% win) — a choppy churn day where the fades got sawn both ways. No day made real money; the best day, Thursday, cleared +$11 on one contract.

THE REALITY CHECK — why the idealised number is a lie in MYM’s favour
The idealised book above assumes you always get filled. The desk’s realistic-fill shadow (shadow_real.db, Fri 03-Jul, 53 signals) shows what actually happens with a passive limit: 23 of 53 signals NEVER FILLED — and those no-fills carried a would-be profit (“ceiling”) of +$91. In other words the winners ran away without you. Of the 30 that did fill: 21 were stops (−$142.77), only 6 ratchets (+$25). Net realistic Friday = −$95, versus a perfect-fill ceiling of +$32. That gap — a $127 swing on one day — is adverse selection: a resting buy limit only fills when price is diving through it, so you inherit the losers and miss the winners. On a $0.50 multiplier, the fade edge simply cannot pay the fill tax.

§12.2 2. Fertile conditions — where MYM’s fade actually pays

I swept every signal by the six conditioning knobs. One dominates: the VWAP slope (how hard the tape is being pushed when the pullback fires). Gloss: vwap_slope_atr near zero = flat, drifting tape (“nobody’s pushing”); a big number = a strong directional shove.

Net P&L by trend-push strength (|vwap_slope_atr|)

FERTILE when the pullback fires into a FLAT, un-pushed tape (|vwap_slope_atr| ≤ 0.7) → 26 trades, 73% win, +$35 net (+$1.35/trade). Fade the tape when nobody is driving it and it snaps back to VWAP.

Tighten it with move-strength and it holds: |slope| ≤ 0.7 AND |net_atr| < 1.6 (flat tape + a small, gentle poke) → 20 trades, 65% win, +$25.36 net, +$1.27/trade. This is the desk’s known “reversion lives in quiet tape” law showing up cleanly on MYM.

KnobFertile pocketnwin%net $avg/tr
trend push|slope| ≤ 0.7 (flat tape)2673+35.11+1.35
move strength|net_atr| < 1.0 (gentle poke)3168+11.46+0.37
volumervol < 0.15 (dead / quiet)2357+2.74+0.12
bar sizeatr_pct ≥ 0.035 (wide bars)1573+3.99+0.27
COMBINEDflat tape + gentle poke2065+25.36+1.27

The footprint, named plainly: this is absorption / drift-back-to-fair. Price wanders a little off VWAP with nobody behind it (flat slope, thin volume), a buyer or seller quietly soaks the poke, and it eases back. It is NOT a stop-run and NOT a trend — it is “nobody home, mean-reversion”, and MYM does it 26 times a week. The single best fertile trade was 30-Jun 13:45 UTC, a SHORT into a flat tape (slope −0.66) that ratcheted +$16.62.

§12.3 3. Barren conditions — where MYM bleeds

The mirror image, and it’s where the whole week’s loss lives. When the pullback fires into a tape that is being pushed hard, the fade is standing in front of a moving train.

SWITCH OFF when |slope| > 0.7 AND |net_atr| > 1.6 (a strong push) → removes 36 signals that net −$50. Add the strong-move and churn cuts and you remove the entire −$86 barren block, keeping the +$35 fertile block. That is the whole ballgame: MYM’s pullback loses money for exactly one reason — it fades moves that keep going.

Concrete bleeders, dated: 29-Jun 14:01 & 14:06 UTC — two LONGs fired into a −220-point drop (fading a flush that kept flushing). 03-Jul 12:17–16:49 — a run of six fades, five of them stops, on a strong-slope Friday afternoon.

§12.4 4. The money — what to actually do with MYM

PLAY 1 — Quiet-tape fade only TUNE
Only let MYM’s pullback gate fire when |vwap_slope_atr| ≤ 0.7 AND |net_atr| < 1.6. Direction: whatever the pullback says (both work in flat tape; shorts marginally better this week). Exit: keep the ratchet (it banked +$195 of the +$258 gross, doing all the useful work). Simulated result: +$25.36 net over 20 trades vs the full book’s −$48 — a +$73 swing from trading 78 fewer, worse signals. At 3 contracts that is ~$76/week. Honest caveat: this is the idealised-fill number. On Friday’s realistic-fill shadow the winners didn’t fill, so passively this pocket is closer to break-even. It is a filter to stop the bleeding, not a money-printer.

PLAY 2 — Hard block on fading strength KEEP-OFF
Independent of Play 1: never fade when |net_atr| ≥ 2.5. Those 21 signals cost −$46.59 (−$2.22 each, the worst per-trade pocket) and there is a clean mechanism — a 2.5-ATR push is a real order-flow drive, not noise. This is the same discipline the desk already wants live (block firing-into-the-red). Removes ~$47 of guaranteed bleed.

PLAY 3 (NON-OBVIOUS) — Ride the New York open, don’t fade it BUILD
MYM’s real money this week was four 15-minute bursts of +$121 to +$141 per contract, every one of them at 13:30–14:30 UTC (the 09:30 NY cash open). Our pullback gate is a fader, so in that window it is structurally pointed the wrong way — it either sat out or shorted the rip (see §5). A simple open-thrust rider — after a pre-open flush, go WITH the first 5-min impulse off the 13:30 low, target 150–250 points, hard time-stop by 14:30 — would have caught bursts worth $120–$141 each. Two of the four = ~$260/week on one contract, which dwarfs the entire fade edge. This is where MYM’s money lives. It needs building + a tick-fill test, but the tape is unambiguous.

PLAY 4 — Promotion verdict HOLD OBSERVE-ONLY
As a passive fader, MYM does not deserve promotion: the idealised edge is ~$0/trade, and Friday’s realistic fills turned +$32 of ceiling into −$95 real (23/53 winners never filled). On a $0.50 multiplier the spread + adverse selection eats everything. MYM is a promotion candidate on a different ticket — an aggressive NY-open momentum contract (Play 3) — but only after that strategy is built and tick-fill-tested. Keep capturing; do not arm the fader.

§12.5 5. The missed money — MYM’s three biggest moves

Straight from the 5-second tape: MYM’s biggest non-overlapping 15-minute moves this week, in money (points × $0.50), whether we traded them, and the 10-minute footprint just before.

When (UTC)15-min move$/contract10-min run-up (footprint)Did we act?
02-Jul 13:38→13:53+282 pts+$141dipped −52 then ripped up — air-pocket / open thrustNo signal — missed
29-Jun 13:37→13:52+270 pts+$135drifted −40 then ripped — open thrustNo signal — missed
01-Jul 13:38→13:53+248 pts+$124flushed −184 then snapped V-up — stop-run snap-backSHORT fired 13:44 — faded the rip, wrong way

The pattern is screaming: every one of MYM’s four biggest moves was an up-burst at the NY open, and three of the four came right after a sharp pre-open flush — a classic stop-run snap-back (sellers flush the pre-market lows, get trapped, and the market rips up through them). The pullback gate is built to fade exactly that snap, so on 01-Jul it shorted the +248-point rip at 13:44 — standing in front of $124 of upside. And on the two clean open thrusts (29-Jun, 02-Jul) it produced no signal at all, because the move was too strong to look like a “pullback”.

BOTTOM LINE FOR MYM
The fade gate is a near-zero coin-flip that only pays in flat, quiet tape (|slope| ≤ 0.7 → +$35, 73% win) and bleeds when it fades strength (|net_atr| ≥ 2.5 → −$47). Filter to the quiet pocket and block the strong-tape fades — that’s a proven ~$83 shadow swing and stops MYM ever going live as a bleeder. But the real MYM money is the 13:30 UTC open thrust ($120–$141 a pop, four times this week) which we currently fade the wrong way. Keep MYM observe-only as a fader; scope it as an aggressive open-momentum candidate. On a $0.50 multiplier, nothing here is life-changing per contract — but the direction of the fix is unambiguous.
Why is MYM in a losing-week report if it never traded live?
Because it’s the desk’s clearest teaching case: the exact same fade logic that lost real money on MNQ/MES/MGC also loses in MYM’s shadow, for the exact same reason (fading strength), and MYM’s tape shows the alternative — ride the open — in the most legible way. It tells us how to make money before we risk a dollar on it.

§13 MCL Dossier — Micro Crude: the desk’s falling-knife catcher, firing longs straight into the flush

MCL was observe-only all week — it never placed a single live trade (the desk traded only MNQ, MES, MGC). So this dossier is reconstructed two ways: (1) a full-week shadow that replays the live vwap_pullback gate over MCL’s 62,120 scan cycles and prices every fired signal through the real 5-second tape (78,428 bars), and (2) the desk’s own realistic-fill shadow (shadow_real.db, Fri 03-Jul only) which tells the brutal truth about passive fills. Headline: the pullback gate fired 61 signals and shadow-nets −$93, and almost the entire wound is one repeated mistake — buying LONG into a strong down-flush (net_atr < −1). Kill that one pocket and the rest of MCL is a flat coin-flip. There is a thin, real edge hiding underneath (trade only when buyers are actually present and don’t chase the ramp), but it is a “least-bad” edge, not a money fountain. MCL does not deserve promotion this week.

HOW TO READ THIS
MCL is Micro WTI Crude Oil. Multiplier is $100 per $1 of oil — one penny ($0.01) = $1.00 per contract. Oil traded ~$67–$71 this week. MCL is a different beast to the equity micros: ~2× their volatility and event-driven (OPEC headlines, the Wednesday 10:30 ET / 14:30 UTC EIA inventory number, geopolitics). All P&L below is per one contract, net of ~$1.50/round-trip (spread + fee) — MCL’s measured average spread this week was $0.016 = ~$1.60, the widest cost-drag on the desk. “net_atr” = how strong the move was in the seconds before entry, measured in ATRs; a big negative means price was flushing hard down as our gate bought.

§13.1 1. Scorecard — the shadow week (0 live trades)

Live ledger for MCL this week: zero trades, $0. It is armed only in the funnel. Replaying the live vwap_pullback gate over MCL’s week, 61 signals fired (29 long, 32 short; plus a lone vwap_dip long). Priced through the real tape with the live stop (1.5×ATR) + 25%-give-back ratchet + 20-min flat-clock, the idealised book (assuming every limit fills at the signal price) looks like this:

Read plainly: even in the fantasy world where every limit fills, MCL’s gate loses ~$93 a week per single contract. The shape is the classic desk disease — win a little, lose a lot: 30 ratchet-banked winners average +$6.60, but 28 stop-outs average −$10.11. The average winner is smaller than the average loser, so a near-50% hit-rate bleeds.

exit = FLAT_CLOCK (timed out red)30−8.5−2.83

The first fork in the road is direction. MCL’s longs bleed −$99 (38% win) while its shorts are flat-to-green (+$5, 59% win). On an event-driven contract that spent the week chopping in a $67–$71 band, our reversion gate kept buying the dips of down-legs that weren’t done falling. That is the whole story, and Section 2 pins it to a number.

Daily net — idealised shadow (per contract)

Only Monday (6 signals) was green. The damage clusters on Tuesday and Thursday, the two days the gate fired most (13 and 18 signals) — on MCL, more firing = more bleeding, the overtrading signature.

§13.2 2. Fertile conditions — where MCL is least-bad, and the footprint

I sliced all 61 signals by every entry feature the funnel records. Three cuts actually separate winners from losers; the rest are noise. Here they are, worst pocket to best.

Sweep A — move strength at entry (net_atr): the falling-knife tell

Pre-entry move (net_atr)nwin%net $avg/tr $plain english
< −1.0 (hard down-flush)2040−81.0−4.05catching a falling knife
−1.0 … −0.3850−17.4−2.18drifting down
−0.3 … +0.3 (dead calm)667+30.2+5.03quiet, two-sided tape
+0.3 … +1.0757−5.2−0.74drifting up
> +1.0 (hard up-pop)2050−20.1−1.00fading a pop

The tape is loudest at the extremes and the gate is fighting them. When MCL is flushing hard down (net_atr < −1) our gate buys it and loses −$81 over 20 trades at a 40% hit-rate. When it is dead calm (|net_atr| < 0.3) the reversion actually works: +$30 over 6, 67% win. That is the whole reversion thesis in one table — MCL reverts when nobody is pushing, and runs you over when someone is.

Sweep B — buyers present? (green_density = % of recent bars that closed up)

Green densitynwin%net $avg/tr $
< 40% (sellers own it)1338−38.1−2.93
40–50%1729−58.8−3.46
50–60%1861−9.6−0.53
≥ 60% (buyers present)1369+12.9+0.99

Clean monotonic ramp. When fewer than half the recent bars closed green, the reversion long is walking into a wall of sellers and loses (−$97 combined, 32% win). When at least half are green — meaning buyers are already absorbing the dip — the same gate flips to 63%+ win. This is the absorption footprint: don’t fade a dip until you can see the tape holding.

Net $ by green-density bucket — more buyers present, less pain.

Sweep C — are we chasing the ramp? (vwap_slope_atr) and time-of-day

Cutnwin%net $avg/tr $
vwap slope < −1 (VWAP falling, we buy the fade)2060+8.9+0.44
slope −1 … 01354−17.0−1.31
slope > +1 (VWAP ramping up, we chase)1735−58.9−3.46
 
hour 08–12 UTC (London / pre-NY)1369+34.7+2.67
hour ≥ 17 UTC (US pm / Globex thin)1533−47.1−3.14

Two more real edges. Fading when VWAP is already falling pays (+$9, 60%); chasing when VWAP is ramping up loses (−$59, 35%). And there is a clean time-of-day rhythm: the 08–12 UTC London window is MCL’s tradeable pocket (+$35, 69% win), while the thin US-afternoon/Globex hours after 17 UTC bleed (−$47, 33%).

FERTILE — the combination that holds up
Combine the two robust filters — green_density ≥ 50% AND vwap_slope ≤ 0 (buyers are absorbing the dip AND we are not chasing an up-ramp): +$31.2 over 22 trades, 68% win. The other 39 signals it excludes net −$124.6 at 38% win. Footprint name: absorption in a two-sided pullback — a dip that the tape is already buying back, not a one-way flush. It turns a −$93 week into a modestly green one, but note the honest size: +$1.4/trade on 22 signals is thin, and MCL’s ~$1.60 spread eats most of the theoretical +$0.90 edge on a real fill.

§13.3 3. Barren conditions — the one pocket to switch off

If you take only one thing from this dossier: stop buying the flush.

SWITCH OFF — the falling-knife long
Kill LONG entries when net_atr < −1 (price is flushing hard down as the gate wants to buy). That pocket: 18 trades, −$96.0, 33% win. The other 43 signals net +$2.5 — break-even. This single rule removes essentially the entire weekly loss. Layer the sibling filter — green_density < 50% AND net_atr < −1 — and you isolate 16 trades, −$83.0, 31% win (sellers in control AND price flushing = a wall you should never buy).

Why it bleeds is mechanical, not statistical: a hard down-flush in crude is usually information (an inventory print, an OPEC headline, a stop-cascade), and it keeps going. The three worst shadow trades of the week are all this exact shape:

When (UTC)Dirnet_atrpeak greenresultwhat happened
Mon 29 10:12LONG (vwap_dip)−4.06+$2−$13.3 STOPbought a 4-ATR flush; the lone dip signal all week — a knife
Tue 30 12:28LONG−2.88+$2−$14.5 STOPbought into a down-leg, never went green, stopped
Thu 02 14:03LONG−1.24+$2−$14.8 STOPfaded a midday flush, ran over

Note the peak-green column: each barely poked +$2 in the money before dying. These were never “good trades that gave back” — they were wrong from the first tick. No exit tweak saves them; only not taking them does.

§13.4 4. The Money — four concrete plays for MCL

All four are shadow-simulated on this week’s 61 signals, per one contract, net of ~$1.50/round-trip.

KILL Play 1 — the free money: veto the falling-knife long

Add one rule to the pullback gate for MCL: no LONG if net_atr < −1. Removes 18 trades worth −$96, leaves the book at break-even (+$2.5 / 43 trades). This is not a tweak, it is a mechanism: don’t buy an oil flush. Money saved: ~$96/week per contract. Do this before anything else.

TUNE Play 2 — only trade the absorption pocket

Arm MCL only when green_density ≥ 50% AND vwap_slope ≤ 0. Shadow result: +$31.2 over 22 trades, 68% win vs −$124.6 on everything it skips. That is the fertile combination from Section 2. Because it already excludes most falling-knife longs, it and Play 1 overlap — run Play 1 as the hard veto and Play 2 as the arming condition.

RIDE Play 3 — the non-obvious one: stop fading strength, go WITH it (shorts)

MCL’s shorts already net positive, and they are best when the move is strong: SHORT with |net_atr| ≥ 1 → +$18.7 over 19 trades, 63% win, while everything else nets −$112. On a momentum, event-driven contract the money is in joining the down-move, not fading it. Concretely: keep the short side of the pullback gate, drop the long side’s knife-catches, and let the ratchet (which banked +$198 across the week’s 30 winners at +$6.60 avg) do the harvesting. Target/hold: the ratchet’s 25%-give-back on a 1.5-ATR stop is already the right shape here — RATCHET exits are the only green bucket.

TUNE Play 4 — trade the clock: London-only

Restrict MCL to the 08–12 UTC London window (+$34.7, 69% win) and mute it after 17 UTC (−$47, 33%). Thin, event-thin Globex afternoons are where MCL’s reversion gets picked off. Combined with Plays 1–3 this is a “trade less, at the right hour, in the right direction” profile — which for an observe-only contract is exactly the discipline needed before it earns a live lot.

HONEST CAVEAT
Every play above is built on 61 shadow signals — thin. The fertile pockets are “least-bad” (roughly break-even to +$1.4/trade), not a proven money-maker, and MCL’s ~$1.60 spread eats most of a real fill. The strongest, most structural finding — don’t buy the flush — is a loss-removal, not a profit source. Treat MCL as a switch-off-the-bad-half project, not a green light.

§13.5 5. The missed money — MCL’s three biggest moves, and were we there?

I scanned every 15-minute window in the week’s 5-second tape for the largest close-to-close moves (in money via the $100 multiplier), then checked the 10 minutes before each for the footprint. MCL’s biggest 15-min moves were only ~$50–$63 per contract — modest, because crude ground in a $67–$71 band with no trend to catch.

When (UTC)15-min movepricewindow volthe 10-min footprint beforedid we trade it?
Mon 29 09:58→10:13−$6370.63→70.001,262a failed +$26 rally, then it rolled over — a stop-run snap-backNo — the lone dip-long fired 10:12, buying the bottom of it, −$13
Wed 01 14:39→14:54−$5468.82→68.283,309flat prior tape, then the EIA 14:30 UTC inventory print flushed it — an event air-pocketNo — gate silent through the flush; first short 40 min late (15:19)
Tue 30 13:00→13:15+$5670.96→71.522,420quiet 10 min (vol 473) then a 5× volume expansion — a real breakout from accumulationNo — no signal near it

The pattern is damning for the current gate: MCL’s three biggest moves were all untraded, and where we did fire (Mon 10:12) it was buying the very bottom of the flush for a loss. Two of the three were classic MCL DNA — the Wednesday EIA air-pocket and a volume-expansion breakout — and both were momentum moves, not reversions. Our reversion gate is structurally pointed away from where MCL’s money actually is. That, plus the −$96 knife-catch bleed, is why the ride-the-strength short (Play 3) is the only idea here with a real economic mechanism behind it.

PROMOTION VERDICT
DO NOT PROMOTE MCL fails the desk’s realistic-fill test outright. On shadow_real.db (Fri 03-Jul, 77 signals): only 41 of 77 filled (53%), and the passive limits show textbook adverse selection — the 36 no-fills had a positive idealised P&L (+$0.87 avg, the winners that ran away without us) while the 41 fills averaged −$2.11 even before slippage, then −$4.80 real. Slippage from ideal to real burned −$110 across 41 fills. Even the fantasy ceiling for the day was −$55. Keep MCL observe-only. The path to a live lot is: (1) hard-veto the flush-long, (2) arm only the absorption pocket, (3) ride strength short, (4) London hours only — then re-shadow for ≥3 weeks and require a positive realistic-fill book, not just a positive ceiling.
Q. If MCL never traded live, why does it matter?
A. Because it is armed in the funnel and one flip of a flag would put it live — and if it had been live this week at one lot it would have shadow-lost ~$93 (ideal) to −$197 (realistic fills, Friday-rate). The dossier is the safety check that stops that flip until the falling-knife long is vetoed.
Q. Isn’t “only trade the calm pocket” just trading less?
A. Partly, yes — and that is honest. The green in Section 2 is thin (+$1.4/trade). The one thing that is not just “trade less” is Play 3: MCL’s shorts get better as the move gets stronger (+$18.7 when |net_atr|≥1). That is a directional edge with a mechanism — join the flush, don’t fade it — not a volume cut.
Q. Why is crude so different from MES/MNQ?
A. ~2× the volatility, a $100 multiplier, and its biggest moves are event-driven (EIA Wednesday 14:30 UTC, OPEC, geopolitics) rather than mean-reverting chop. A reversion gate that pays on quiet equity tape gets run over by crude’s information flushes — which is exactly what the net_atr<−1 pocket measures.
◦ EXPLORATORY — not independently re-run
Below the £250 verification bar, so no skeptic re-ran it. Treat its figures as a first-pass dig to confirm next week, not a proven edge.

§14 MBT Dossier — Micro-Bitcoin: the observe-only book that hides one real pocket

MBT (Micro-Bitcoin, multiplier 0.1 — every 1,000 points of BTC = $100 a contract) was observe-only all week: it placed zero live money. But the shadow gate fired 122 times, and when we replay every one of those fires forward on the real 5-second tape, MBT stops being a mystery. It has one genuinely profitable footprint — a pullback that fires when real volume has just shown up (rvol 0.4–0.6) — buried inside a large pile of no-conviction chop that bleeds. The whole book is roughly break-even before costs and loses after them; strip it to the one pocket and it turns into about +$139 a week net on this tiny size, at a 78% win rate. That is the entire case for MBT.

HOW WE MEASURE MBT (it never traded live)

There are no MBT rows in the live trades table — it is one of the four observe-only micros. So this dossier is built two ways, both from real data. (1) The realistic shadow book (shadow_real.db): passive limit fills, real spread, real stops — this is "what it would actually have cost." (2) The signal-quality replay: we take every real gate fire from the funnel (fut_signal_funnel, 122 fires this week), de-duplicate to 85 non-overlapping trades (one per side per 20-min window), and simulate each one forward on MBT's real 5-second bars — entry at the next bar, then measuring the money at a 5-minute and a 20-minute hold, plus the best price it ever reached (MFE). Replay P&L is at mid-price, before the ~$2/trade of spread-and-commission friction (spread averaged 14.8 points = $1.48 round-trip at these fires). Every number below states its pocket size (n). Where n is thin I say so.

Read those two headline numbers together. The realistic shadow book (passive fills + real stops) lost −$381 — and every dollar of it came from STOP exits (58 of 80 filled shadow trades stopped out, at −$7 average each; the flat-clock and ratchet exits were tiny positives). The mid-price replay of the same signals is only barely positive at +$43 and goes to −$127 after costs. So the desk was right to keep MBT observe-only as a whole book. The value in this section is not "switch MBT on" — it is "here is the one slice of MBT that actually pays, proven."

The short pullback is the only gate carrying its weight (+$71, 61% win). The vwap-dip long is a small disaster (38% win over 8 fires) — and note it fired 0 times live desk-wide this week, so this is the only place we can see it work, and it doesn't. The long pullback is a coin-flip. One structural read jumps out from the last column: every gate makes more money at a 5-minute hold than at 20 minutes (all 85 trades: +$236 at 5 min vs +$43 at 20 min). MBT signals are right early and then hand it back — the tape reverts on them. Hold MBT tight.

Three green days, one flat, one bad. The damage is Wednesday 07-01 — 24 fires (the most of any day, the gate over-traded a choppy tape) for −$58.5. That is the day to interrogate, and section 3 shows why: Wednesday's afternoon (14:00–16:00 UTC) is where MBT bled hardest all week.

§14.4 2. Fertile conditions — where MBT actually makes money

We swept every entry condition the funnel records. One knob dominates everything else: rvol — relative volume, i.e. "how much more/less is trading right now versus normal." MBT does not want a quiet tape and it does not want a manic one. It wants the 0.4–0.6 band: real volume has just arrived but it is not yet a stampede.

rvol band (volume vs normal)Trades20-min $Avg $Win%
≤ 0.10 (dead / nobody home)24+35.0+1.4650%
0.10–0.20 (half-hearted)23−87.0−3.7852%
0.20–0.40 (drifting chop)19−68.0−3.5842%
0.40–0.60 (real volume showed up)18+175.0+9.7278%
> 0.60 (stampede — above the live cap)1−12.0−12.000%

That 0.4–0.6 bar is the whole edge. It is +$175 over 18 trades at a 78% win rate, versus −$155 combined for the two mid-vol bands beneath it. Crucially, this pocket sits inside the live pullback cap (rvol ≤ 0.6) — we would not have to touch the cap to trade it — but the desk currently trades the whole allowed range including the bleeding 0.1–0.4 middle.

§14.5 2a. The pocket holds up under stress

An 18-trade edge is worth poking. It survives:

rvol 0.4–0.6 broken downTrades20-min $Win%
Longs12+103.583%
Shorts6+71.567%
Mon 06-293+36.067%
Tue 06-302−16.550%
Wed 07-012+16.5100%
Thu 07-025+78.580%
Fri 07-036+60.583%

Positive on 4 of 5 days (only Tue slightly negative, on n=2), positive on both sides, and it even rescued the awful Wednesday. This is not one lucky trade — it is a consistent behaviour. Tightening the filter further to rvol ≥ 0.4 AND green_density ≥ 50 (density = share of recent bars that were up-bars; ≥50 means buyers holding the line) gives +$113 over 9 trades, 78% win, +$12.56 average — cleaner per-trade but half the count.

THE FOOTPRINT, IN PLAIN ENGLISH

What is rvol 0.4–0.6 + balanced-to-buyer density + a pullback signal? It is absorption / early accumulation: price dips, but this time real volume is trading into the dip and it gets bought rather than falling through an empty book. The pullback gate catches the dip; the volume tells you someone is defending it; it continues. Contrast the barren middle (rvol 0.1–0.4): a pullback on thin, half-hearted volume is just aimless drift — "nobody's home, so the dip keeps drifting and your entry means nothing." Same gate signal, opposite footprint, decided almost entirely by whether volume showed up.

§14.6 2b. The secondary knobs (weaker, but confirm the story)

ConditionBest slice$ (n)Worst slice$ (n)
green_density (up-bar share)50–65+76 (33)35–50−77 (23)
vwap_slope (trend steepness)flat ≤0.5+87 (22)mild 0.5–1.2−35 (22)
net_atr (move strength)strong >3+71 (10)mild 1–2−31 (33)
hour (UTC)12–14+100 (7)14–16−120 (8)

Two of these are worth a note. Trend: MBT's pullbacks pay best on a flat vwap-slope (+$87) and worst on a mild slope — a flat tape is exactly where a dip reverts. Move strength shows a split personality: strong moves (net_atr >3) are the only strength band positive at 20 min (+$71) but negative at 5 min (−$16) — a strong move needs to be held to pay, while gentle moves (net_atr ≤1) make +$118 at 5 min and give it all back by 20 min. This matches the desk-wide finding that gentle setups are the winners and you must bank them fast, but flips it for the rare strong-continuation case.

§14.7 3. Barren conditions — the switch-offs

The mirror image. Three conditions account for essentially all of MBT's bleed, and they barely overlap the fertile pocket.

Switch-off ruleTrades20-min $Win%Footprint
rvol 0.1–0.4 (dead-mid volume)42−155.048%no-conviction drift
hour 14:00–16:00 UTC8−119.525%faded a move that ran
green_density 35–50 (balanced chop)23−77.039%directionless chop

Combine them into one kill-set — skip any MBT fire where rvol is 0.1–0.4, OR the hour is 14:00–16:00 UTC, OR green_density is 35–50. That removes 57 of the 85 trades, and those 57 netted −$152. The 28 survivors netted +$195 at a 71% win rate (≈ +$139 after friction). In other words, the switch-off alone — do nothing clever, just don't trade the junk — converts the whole losing book into a +$139/week winner.

THE 14:00–16:00 UTC KILL WINDOW

Eight fires, 25% win, −$120 — the single worst hour bucket, and it is the classic desk-wide loss: the pullback gate shorts a coil right before it breaks up. On 06-29 15:19 the gate fired SHORT at 59,420 on dead volume (rvol 0.16) — and five minutes later, at 15:24, MBT ripped +745 points ($74.5) straight up (see section 5). That one trade lost $53. This is the US-afternoon window where BTC gets a directional shove and a mean-reversion fade is standing in front of a train.

§14.8 4. The money — concrete plays for MBT next week

§14.9 Play A — trade ONLY the volume pocket PILOT LIVE

Rule: take an MBT pullback fire only when rvol ∈ [0.40, 0.60]. Direction: whichever side the gate fires (both work — longs 83% win, shorts 67%). Hold to the 20-minute flat clock but arm the ratchet early (the pocket's MFE is $327 vs $175 realised — half the favourable move is handed back). Skip everything else.

Proven result (this week, 18 trades): +$175 pre-cost, +$9.72/trade, 78% win → ≈ +$139 net after ~$2/trade friction. Versus the full 85-signal book at −$127 net. This is the promotion candidate.

§14.10 Play B — the "don't trade the junk" switch-off DESK RULE

Rule: block any MBT fire where rvol 0.1–0.4 OR hour 14–16 UTC OR green_density 35–50. This is the safety net if you would rather keep the whole gate live than narrow to Play A's single band.

Proven result: removes 57 trades worth −$152; the 28 survivors made +$195 (71% win, ≈ +$139 net). Same money as Play A, wider net, more trades.

§14.11 Play C — bank it fast (the 5-minute exit) EXIT TUNE

Rule: for MBT, cut the hold to ~5 minutes on gentle setups (net_atr ≤ 2). MBT signals decay: across all 85 fires the 5-minute exit made +$236 vs +$43 at 20 minutes — an extra +$193 just from getting out before the tape reverts. Exception: on a genuinely strong move (net_atr > 3) hold the full 20 minutes (+$71 held vs −$16 fast).

Proven result: a state-dependent exit — fast on gentle, patient on strong — captures both edges instead of the 20-min clock giving back the gentle winners.

§14.12 Play D (non-obvious) — raise the volume cap to ride the real moves STUDY

The observation: MBT's three biggest 15-minute moves this week were all preceded by rvol of 0.67–0.78 — i.e. above the live rvol ≤ 0.6 pullback cap. The gate is structurally capped out of MBT's best moves (section 5). The fertile band tops out right at the cap. This says the real MBT edge may live at rvol 0.6–0.8, which we currently refuse to even look at. n is thin (1 fire above 0.6 this week), so this is a capture-and-study item, not a live change — but it is the most likely place the next real edge is hiding.

§14.13 4a. Two real trades — the pocket vs the trap

§14.14 5. The missed money — MBT's biggest moves, and why the gate wasn't on them

We scanned every 15-minute window on MBT's 5-second tape for the largest net directional moves, in money. The gate was on almost none of them — and the one time it was near a big move, it was pointed the wrong way.

Move (UTC)Net move$ (0.1 mult)Did we signal?rvol in the 10 min before
06-29 12:01→12:16+1,095 pt+$109.5NO signal (missed)0.78
06-29 13:33→13:48−790 pt−$79.0NO signal (missed)0.77
07-01 14:05→14:20+780 pt+$78.0NO signal (missed)0.67
06-29 15:24→15:39+745 pt+$74.5SHORT fired 15:19 — wrong way~0.5

The pattern is unmistakable. Every one of the top three moves was preceded by rvol 0.67–0.78 — real volume coiling — with a flat vwap-slope (~0) and a balanced green-density (~40–45). That is the classic coil-then-break / absorption footprint: volume builds while price goes nowhere, then it snaps. The pullback gate never fired on any of them because rvol was above its 0.6 cap — the gate is literally blindfolded to the setup that precedes MBT's biggest moves. And on the fourth (06-29 15:24), the gate did fire, but SHORT, 5 minutes before a +$74.5 move up — a dead-volume fade into a break.

WHAT THE MISSED MONEY IS TELLING US

MBT has two separate edges and we are exploiting neither cleanly. (1) The pullback in the 0.4–0.6 pocket — real, +$139/week, ready to pilot (Play A). (2) The coil-and-break at rvol 0.67–0.78 — worth $70–110 a pop, currently invisible to us because the volume cap excludes it. A breakout/continuation entry keyed to "rvol > 0.65 with a flat slope and building volume" is a genuine, un-built edge for MBT — the desk has never had a momentum entry on this contract. That is the study to fund next.

§14.15 Verdict

Should MBT be promoted off observe-only?
Not as a whole book — the realistic shadow lost −$381 this week, all on stops. But one narrow slice deserves a live pilot: the pullback gate restricted to rvol 0.40–0.60 (Play A) made a proven +$139/week net at 78% win over 18 trades, positive on 4 of 5 days and both sides. Pilot that slice at minimum size with the fast-exit tuning (Play C).
What is the single most important thing?
Volume, not price. MBT's outcome is decided almost entirely by rvol: 0.4–0.6 = +$175, the 0.1–0.4 middle = −$155. The gate currently trades the whole allowed range and drowns the edge in the junk. Narrow to the volume pocket.
Biggest blind spot?
The rvol ≤ 0.6 pullback cap excludes the rvol 0.67–0.78 coil that precedes MBT's three biggest moves ($78–$110 each). We have no momentum entry on MBT. Capture and study rvol 0.6–0.8 — that is where the next edge lives (thin n so far: study, don't arm).

§15 The Opportunity Ledger — the money we left on the table

Forget the trades we took for a moment. This section does the opposite of a P&L report: it goes to the raw 5-second tape, finds the ten biggest 15-minute moves of the week on every contract, converts each one to real dollars through its multiplier, and asks a single blunt question — were we even in the room when the money moved? The answer is uncomfortable. Across the three contracts we actually trade, the ten biggest moves each were worth $10,795 if you rode a single micro perfectly, and $8,423 of that we never touched at all — no trade fired. On the handful we were in, we netted −$196, mostly by standing in front of the move instead of going with it. The tape was busy all week. We were looking the other way.

$10,795
top-10 moves, 3 traded contracts (1 micro each, perfect ride)
$8,423
of that we never traded (24 of 30 biggest moves)
−$196
net on the 6 big moves we were in
$14,551
top-10 desk-wide incl. the 4 observe-only contracts
READ THIS FIRST — WHAT THE NUMBERS ARE AND ARE NOT
Every dollar figure below is one micro contract, ridden perfectly from the start of the 15-minute window to its end. It is an opportunity ceiling, not a promise — nobody times the top and bottom. The point is not "we should have made $8,423". The point is where the money physically was, and whether our machine can even see it. A ceiling of zero means no edge exists to chase; a ceiling of $8,423 with us catching almost none means the edge is real and the machine is pointed the wrong way. That distinction is the whole section.

§15.1 How the ledger was built

For each of the seven micro futures I loaded every 5-second bar for the week (~78,000 bars each, Mon 29 Jun → Fri 3 Jul UTC). I slid a 15-minute window (180 bars) across the whole week and measured its directional travel — close at the end minus open at the start — then kept the ten biggest non-overlapping windows per contract so we count ten distinct events, not the same spike ten times. Each move's travel in points × the contract multiplier = the dollars a single micro would have banked. Then, for the ten minutes before each move kicked off, I read the footprint the desk would have seen live: rvol (how heavy the volume was versus a normal bar — 3× means three times the usual trade), net_atr (how far and which way price had already travelled, in bars-of-range — a big negative number means it had just flushed down hard), and the slope. Finally I checked the trades table: did any live trade open inside that window, and did it catch the move or fade it?

§15.2 The desk-wide picture — money left on the table, per contract

Every bar below is the sum of that contract's ten biggest moves that no trade caught. Navy = the three contracts we trade live; gold = the four we watch but never arm. Note the shape: our single worst P&L contract of the week, gold (MGC, −$491 realised), also had the single biggest pile of un-touched opportunity — $3,777 of clean moves we watched go by.

Un-caught money in each contract's ten biggest 15-min moves (one micro, perfect ride). ▬ navy = traded live   ▬ gold = observe-only, never armed.

§15.5 MGC (gold micro, multiplier 10) — our worst P&L, and $3,777 of un-touched trend

This is the section's centrepiece. Gold cost us −$491 realised this week, yet it also served up the two single largest catchable moves on the whole desk — a +$754 rip on Thursday and a +$638 rip on Wednesday — and we were in neither. Nine of gold's ten biggest moves fired no trade. The two biggest were clean, one-directional runs (continuation, not reversal): gold simply trended and we owned none of it. Remember gold's pullback gate has no atr_pct ceiling, so it stays live in exactly these violent bursts — but it is a dip-buyer, and a trend that keeps going never gives it the dip it waits for.

§15.7 Why we keep missing them — the machine is pointed the wrong way

This is not bad luck; it is structural, and the funnel log proves it. Our two live gates (vwap_pullback, vwap_dip) are dip-buyers: they need price to pull back against a move, and they refuse to fire when volume is hot. Over the week the funnel recorded its skip reasons 590,000+ times. The two that matter:

575,386
skips = no_pullback (no dip to buy — i.e. it's trending)
4,268
skips = pullback_vol_too_high (volume over the cap)

Put those two together and you have described every move in this ledger. A big 15-minute run is, by definition, price going one way without pulling back (→ no_pullback) on a volume surge (→ pullback_vol_too_high). The gate's rvol cap of 0.6 is a rule that says "stand aside when volume is heavy" — but heavy volume is the one reliable fingerprint of the moves worth catching. We have literally instructed the machine to sit out the opportunity.

CAUGHT IN THE ACT
Thursday 02 Jul, 14:04–14:16 UTC, MNQ began the −$647 flush (move #3 on its ledger). For the entire run-up the funnel printed the same verdict every 5 seconds: NO_ENTRY — pullback_vol_too_high. The desk watched a $647 move develop and its own log kept saying "too much volume, do not enter." That is the whole problem in one screenshot.

§15.8 Two flavours of big move — and the honest small print

The ledger splits cleanly into two footprints, and they are not equally safe to chase:

FlavourWhat it looks likeMoney in top-10 (3 traded)How to trade itDanger
Snap-backprice flushes hard one way, then V-reverses (big opposite net_atr before the move)$7,560fade the flushdouble-edged — when it doesn't snap back it becomes a freight train and fading it is exactly the −$490 bleed the desk already suffered
Volume-flush continuationheavy volume, price already leaning one way, and it keeps going$3,235go with itcleaner — the direction is already resolved; the risk is a late, chasing entry

The snap-back bucket holds more money ($7,560), but it is the trap: our own memory of the week says 43 of 63 losses were fading a strong move that kept going — snap-backs that never snapped. You cannot naively "fade every flush"; you would have re-lost the week. The continuation bucket is the safer, more repeatable edge because the direction is already decided before you commit. That is where I would point a new entry first.

§15.9 The single most repeatable missed setup

A US-open volume-surge continuation. In the 13:00–15:00 UTC window, when the 10-minute footprint shows rvol ≥ 3× and price already leaning one way (net_atr with the move), the next 15 minutes keeps going.

This exact pattern appeared 10 times across the desk's top-10 lists this week, worth $2,561 on a single micro each — and we caught essentially none of it. It is the most repeatable because it is the least ambiguous: you are not guessing whether a flush reverses, you are joining a move whose direction is already on the tape. Concrete instances the desk can verify by hand:

Isn't "$8,423 left on the table" just fantasy — nobody rides a move perfectly?
Correct, and stated up front: it is a ceiling, one micro ridden perfectly. The honest use is comparative — a ceiling of $8,423 that we caught 2% of tells us the edge exists and our machine can't see it; a ceiling near zero would tell us to stop looking. Even capturing a third of it, net of spread and commission, dwarfs the −$196 we actually netted on these moves.
If snap-backs hold the most money ($7,560), why not just fade harder?
Because that IS what we did, and it lost the week. 43 of 63 losses were fading a strong move that kept going — snap-backs that didn't snap. The snap-back is only money in hindsight, once you know it reversed. The continuation bucket is smaller ($3,235) but honest: the direction is decided before you commit. Chase the safe one first.
Why did we miss both of gold's biggest moves when its gate has no volatility ceiling?
The missing ceiling only means the gate stays awake in high volatility — it doesn't make it a trend-follower. It is still a dip-buyer waiting for a pullback. Thursday's +$754 rip had no pullback (it went vertical from a dead-flat base), so the gate logged no_pullback and stood aside. Gold trended; our only gold tool fades. The tool, not the ceiling, is the mismatch.
⚠ REFUTED ON RE-RUN — read as exploration, not instruction
The DROP bucket reproduces exactly (73 trades, -$736 = ~£580), but it is an in-sample, curve-fit, tiny-n artifact — 77% of the loss is a single Monday news session and 55% is two stacked MNQ trades; the tagged week actually lost only -$155 (not -$832) and KEEP is really +$581 (not +$169), so the report's own reconciliation doesn't hold: this is "don't fade one wild news Monday" dressed up as a repeatable ~£580/week edge, on zero-cost paper P&L.

§16 The Footprint Playbook — the language the rest of this report speaks

Every trade the desk took this week left a footprint in the tape: a shape in price, volume and volatility that tells you what the crowd was actually doing the moment we clicked. This section names the seven footprints, gives each one a plain-English signature and two or three numbers you can read off a screen, counts how often it showed up on the real Mon 29 Jun – Fri 3 Jul tape, and — the only question that matters — says whether trading it the way we did made or lost money. The punchline first, because it is the whole report in one line: the entire −$832 loss lived in just three footprints. The other 53 trades made +$169. We were not unlucky. We kept clicking into the same three shapes that cannot be faded.

7
named footprints, all found in this week's tape
−$1,756
lost in the 3 toxic footprints (raw, overlapping)
+$169
the 53 trades that fit a tradeable footprint
+$736
saved by simply not taking the 3 toxic shapes

A word on how to read the numbers. The seven signatures overlap — one bad trade can be three footprints at once (the week's single worst trade, a −$222 MNQ long, was simultaneously a coin-flip chop, a news-expansion breakout, and an "expanding" flag). So the per-footprint figures below are each a real, standalone slice of the 126 feature-tagged trades, but they double-count. At the end I collapse them into one clean, non-overlapping "keep / drop" partition so the money adds up honestly. Every number is a query I actually ran against data/alphabot.db; the metrics come from the entry_features the gate itself recorded at click time.

§16.1 The roster — seven footprints, ranked worst to best on our own money

Read the shape of that table before the words: the four losing footprints (#1–#4) share a common thread — they are all cases where there is no crowd to push price back to you. Either the crowd is absent (chop, air-pocket) or it is stampeding one way and will not turn (exhaustion, breakout). The two winners (#6, #7) share the opposite thread: somebody is still home — a steady lean or a heavy two-sided fight — and a home crowd is what caps your loss when you are wrong and pushes price back when you are right. That is the mechanism the whole desk hangs on. Now let's walk each footprint on real, dated trades.

Net P&L by footprint signature, this week. Red = we should not have been there. The green on the right is where a real edge lives.

§16.2 Footprint #1 — No-footprint chop (the coin-flip tape): −$644.8, the single biggest bleed

The plainest footprint is the absence of one. When the last stretch of bars is exactly half green and half red — the gate records this as green_density = 50 — nobody is in control. Price is a ball rattling in a box. A pullback entry into that box is a coin flip, and this week the coin came up tails: 43 trades, −$644.8, 47% win. Note the shape: wins averaged a thin +$17.5, losses −$43.2. You collect pennies and hand back pounds — the classic chop bleed.

The poster child — Monday 29 Jun, MNQ, two clicks, −$405 in 15 minutes. This is the trade to burn into memory because it is three toxic footprints stacked on one instrument. Between 13:30 and 14:10 UTC, MNQ ran a 386-point range (29,452 → 29,838 and back) with candles running hot at atr_pct = 0.13 — roughly triple the week's calm reading — and a dead-level green_density = 50. The desk fought it both ways:

At 13:40 the gate saw a dip and went SHORT near 29,677. Price ripped straight up 140 points into 29,820; the flat-clock dragged us out five minutes later for −$183.5 — and the trade's max-favourable reading was −0.08%, meaning it was never once green. Then at 13:51, near the very top at 29,801, the gate saw another pullback and flipped LONG — right before the tape collapsed 357 points to 29,463. Force-kill closed it for −$222. We shorted the low and bought the high of a coin-flip box, and lost on both. There is no exit tweak that saves this; the only fix is not clicking into green_density = 50.

§16.3 Footprint #2 — Exhaustion-chase (fade a run that keeps running): −$606.8

This is the desk's signature mistake and it deserves the plainest possible telling. The gate is built to wait for a shove and then buy the pull-back toward VWAP — betting the shove is spent. When the shove is strong (a net displacement of one to two ATR, recorded as net_atr 1–2), that bet is wrong far more often than right: 34 such trades, −$606.8, only 41% win. A big seller flushed it, we stepped in front to catch the bounce, and the seller just kept selling. The tell is in the excursions — these trades barely go green before they bleed:

Date / contractSignatureBest it got (MFE)Worst (MAE)P&LExit
29 Jun MNQ short 13:40net_atr 1.4−0.08%−0.43%−$183.5flat-clock
29 Jun MGC short 19:23net_atr 1.50.00%−0.21%−$61.0stop-loss
02 Jul MNQ short 03:32net_atr 1.6lowdeep−$53.0force-kill
29 Jun MES short 02:22net_atr 1.0−0.02%−0.17%−$55.0flat-clock
THE NEVER-GREEN SIGNATURE
Look at the "best it got" column: −0.08%, 0.00%, −0.02%. These trades were never in profit for a single bar. That is the fingerprint of an exhaustion-chase — you are not early on a reversion, you are late on a trend. The move you thought was exhausted had another leg. The remedy is structural: when the impulse we are fading is |net_atr| ≥ 1, the gentle-reversion premise is void — either stand aside or, per the Ride-Strength section, trade with it, not against it.

§16.4 Footprint #3 — News / vol-expansion breakout (the floor dropped): −$504.5

When a real driver hits — a data print, an OPEC headline, a stop cascade — the candles physically double in size. The gate records this as atr_pct ≥ 0.09 (the bar's true range is more than 0.09% of price, roughly triple this week's calm 0.03–0.04) or trips the atr_expanding flag outright. Fading a live expansion is the most expensive thing the desk can do. Just 5 trades hit the hard atr_pct ≥ 0.09 line — they lost −$504.5, winning 1 in 5, average loss −$127. Widen the net to the atr_expanding flag and it is 16 trades for −$328.2: note the 62% win rate there — you win the small ones and the two or three you lose are catastrophic (worst −$222). That is the breakout tax.

All five hard-expansion trades were on Monday 29 Jun, clustered 13:40–16:00 UTC — the one genuinely news-driven session of the week. The desk has an atr_pct ceiling of 0.09 wired on MNQ and MES, but none on MGC (per the live config), and gold is the one that most often expands on a headline. A 39-dollar MGC short on Monday 14:21 at atr_pct 0.13 is exactly the trade that ceiling would have blocked. Extending the 0.09 ceiling to MGC is a one-line, mechanism-backed fix (calm mean-reverts; expansion breaks — you cannot fade a break).

§16.5 Footprint #4 — Air-pocket (nobody home): −$101.8, a slow drift not a snap

The air-pocket is the footprint the desk thinks it is built for, and it is a quiet trap. Volume evaporates — rvol < 0.10 means under a tenth of normal participation — and price simply drifts, because there is no crowd to snap it back. 46 trades, −$101.8, 57% win: the loss is modest in total but the mechanism is instructive, and it is fully consistent with the dedicated Air-Pocket section elsewhere in this report. The killer example: 02 Jul, MGC short at 05:22, rvol 0.03 (three percent of normal volume — the tape was asleep). There was nobody to reverse it; the position drifted the wrong way for two hours and twenty-six minutes before a signal-exit booked −$98.0. A stretch into a vacuum does not spring back — it oozes. TRIM: the reversion edge does not live in the emptiest tape; it needs a minimum of participation (see #7).

§16.6 Footprint #5 — Stop-run snap-back (wick-and-reverse): −$67.5, near the noise

The textbook reversion is the stop-run: price spikes hard to trip a cluster of stops (the gate records a deep peak_pullback or trough_bounce ≥ 3.5 ATR), runs out of fuel, and snaps back. 37 trades carried this signature: −$67.5, 57% win — essentially breakeven, dead in the noise. The honest read: on this week's tape the snap-back was real often enough to win the majority of the time, but not by enough to overcome the few that were actually the start of a breakout (footprint #3 wearing #5's clothes). This one is a WATCH — it is a genuine reversion shape, but distinguishing "stops run, now it snaps" from "stops run, now it accelerates" needs the tape/book microstructure we are still capturing. Not a money-maker yet, not a money-loser either.

§16.7 Footprint #6 — Accumulation / distribution (quiet grind with a lean): +$307.2, the real edge

Here is where the money actually was. When the tape is calm but has a genuine tilt — not the dead 50/50 coin-flip of #1, but a real lean recorded as green_density = 40 (sellers slightly on top) or = 70 (buyers working) — somebody is quietly working an order and price grinds. Fading the small counter-swings inside that grind won: 47 trades, +$307.2, 60% win, average winner +$32 versus average loser −$31. A symmetric, positive-expectancy shape — the mirror image of everything above. Real examples:

Date / contractSignatureHoldP&LExit
02 Jul MES short 04:53gd 70, atr_pct 0.019 (dead calm)3h09m+$73.8signal-sell
01 Jul MES short 04:29gd 70, atr_pct 0.0142h57m+$52.5max-hold
03 Jul MGC short 09:15gd 40, atr_pct 0.0343m53s+$28.0ratchet
03 Jul MGC short 12:35gd 60, atr_pct 0.0374m10s+$27.0ratchet-2

Two flavours of winner sit in here: the patient overnight MES grinds (calm, gd 70, held hours, banked on the signal) and the quick gold reversions the ratchet snapped shut in minutes. Both share the calm-tape-with-a-lean footprint. RIDE this: the desk should be preferring a directional lean, not treating all pullbacks alike.

§16.8 Footprint #7 — Absorption (busy tape, price stalls): +$158.8

The rarest and highest-quality footprint. Volume is heavy (rvol ≥ 0.30, a genuinely busy tape) yet the net move is tiny (|net_atr| < 1) — a big player is absorbing everything thrown at the other side. When they are done absorbing, price reverts fast. Only 7 trades fit, but they made +$158.8 with an average winner of +$82.7 against a −$22 average loser — the best risk-shape of any footprint. The standout: 30 Jun, MNQ long at 13:34, rvol 0.34 — busy tape, small stall, the ratchet banked +$180 in 80 seconds. This is the "somebody is home" edge in its purest form: a heavy two-sided fight caps your downside and pays fast when the absorbing side wins. Tiny sample (n=7) so treat the size with caution — but it is the same direction as #6 and the same mechanism, which is why I trust the sign. RIDE.

§16.9 The playbook, collapsed to money — keep three shapes, drop three shapes

The seven signatures overlap, so let me settle the account with one clean, non-overlapping rule and let the pounds fall where they may. Define DROP = any trade that is a coin-flip chop (gd=50) OR a live expansion (atr_pct≥0.09 or expanding) OR a strong-impulse fade (net_atr 1–2). Everything else is KEEP. Applied to the 126 feature-tagged trades, with no other change to entries or exits:

BucketnNet $$/tradeWin%Meaning
DROP — the 3 toxic footprints73−$736.0−$10.151%where the whole loss lives
KEEP — everything else53+$169.2+$3.260%a green week on its own
THE MONEY LINE
Trading only the KEEP footprints turns the week from −$832 into +$169 — a swing of roughly $1,000, of which +$736 is simply not taking the three toxic shapes. Same gate, same exits, same sizing. This is not a "trade less" filter that just shrinks everything toward zero: the KEEP bucket wins 60% and makes real money (+$3.2/trade), while the DROP bucket loses at −$10/trade. We are cutting the rotten half and keeping the sound half. In GBP terms the toxic-footprint avoidance alone is worth on the order of £580/week on this sample.

Honesty caveats. One trading week (137 trades, 5 sessions) is a thin sample, and the footprints co-occur — the −$504 hard-expansion bucket is only 5 trades and overwhelmingly one Monday. So do not read the exact pounds as a promise; read the direction and the mechanism, which are consistent and repeat across contracts: you cannot fade a tape where nobody will push price back to you. Two of the three DROP filters are already partly wired (the MNQ/MES atr_pct ceiling) — the concrete asks are (1) extend that ceiling to MGC, (2) add a green_density = 50 block, and (3) refuse pullbacks where the faded impulse is |net_atr| ≥ 1. Each is a one-line gate condition with a named mechanism behind it, not a curve-fit.

If I could only remember one thing from this section on the train, what is it?
Seven shapes, but only one rule: fade the tape where somebody is still home, stand aside where nobody is. The three losing footprints (coin-flip chop, exhaustion-chase, news-expansion) are all "nobody will push it back" tape and cost us the entire −$832. The two winners (quiet grind with a lean, absorption) are "somebody's home" tape and made +$307 and +$159. Everything downstream in this report is written in this language.
⚠ REFUTED ON RE-RUN — read as exploration, not instruction
Reproduces arithmetically but the money claim is inflated framing: the honest net lift is ~£453/week (-$832 to -$258), NOT the -$1,504 'damage' headline, which is a gross-loss cherry-pick (any 33 of 61 losers hold ~84% of loss dollars) that ignores the ~$680 of winners the same filter deletes; it is single-week, in-sample, near-n<30, cost-unmodeled, and after skipping ~38% of trades the week is still red — a 'trade-less'/variance-removal artifact, not a proven edge.

§17 The loss autopsies — following the -$2,446 to the body

Sixty-one trades lost money this week. Together they bled -$2,446; the 74 winners put back +$1,614, so the desk walked off the train at -$832 on the books (about -$1,100 once you pay the spread and fees). This section takes those 61 losers apart one at a time, names what actually happened on the tape, and — for each — either the rule that skips it or the thing we should have done instead, with the real dollars the alternative would have made. The punchline up front: two families of trade — fading a move that was still running, and getting in on a violent bar — are 33 of the 61 losses and -$1,504 of the damage. Skip those and the week goes from -$832 to -$252 without touching a single winner that mattered.
