§30.2 2. Dissecting the precursor — two animals, one catchable

Slicing the 20 minutes before each move on 5s bars + the funnel features (rvol, atr_pct, vwap_slope_atr, book_imbalance) splits the eight cleanly:

Family A — the volume-surge break (the up-runs #5, #6, #8, and the down-continuation #4). Catchable. The fingerprint: the tape goes quiet and compressed, then a burst of participation drives price out of the recent range and it keeps going.

  • 07-01 13:45 (+$101) is the storybook case. Price coiled ~3032–3037 for twelve minutes on single-digit volume (atr_pct 0.056 — volatility crushed), printing a staircase of higher-lows. Then rvol ramped to 3.2× and the last five minutes traded 16× the volume of the five before them; a 377-contract 5s bar at 14:00 detonated the continuation.
  • 07-02 13:38 (+$101) is the same at higher octane: the last five minutes before the break traded 7.0× the prior five (75 vs 11 contracts/bar), rvol 2.48, price sitting at the bottom of its range (pos-in-range 0.13) — accumulation, then lift.
  • Crucially, on #6 the funnel's own vwap_slope_atr read −0.27 and net_atr −0.39 as price broke up. The desk's trend read pointed the wrong way — which is exactly why the fader gates and the trend-shadow miss it. The volume surge is the only thing that saw it coming.

Family B — the exhaustion reversal (the biggest down-moves #2, #3, #7 and the pop #1). Not catchable by a volume trigger. Before #3 (−$117) price was pinned at the top of its range (pos 0.85) and rolled over on 0.66× the prior volume; #4's roll-over came on 0.73×, rvol 0.53. There is no participation spike to key on — buyers simply evaporate and price sags. A momentum trigger arrives late and wrong; these are the faders' own turf, or thin-tape news.

The precursor, drawn: 07-01 13:45–14:00, coil then surge

Blue = 5s close; grey = per-bar volume; yellow band = the quiet higher-low coil; red dashed = the 14:00 volume surge (377 contracts) that carried price +20 pt (+$101). Note the volume floor through the coil and the spike at the break.

§30.3 3. The microstructure gave nothing — and that matters

I checked the 250 ms quotes (data/ticks.db) for a leading tell around the tick-covered moves. There is none. Spread sat at a flat ~0.17 pt before, during and after both the up-break (#5) and the roll-over (#4) — it does not widen into a move. Book sizes hovered near 4×4 with no persistent imbalance (bid 4.09 vs ask 3.87 before the up-run — a whisper, not a signal). On the thinnest book of the desk, the 250 ms tape is too sparse to lead; the only precursor that survives is the 5s volume surge. That also means costs are honest and small — a 0.17 pt spread + fees is well under the 0.4 pt ($2) round-trip I charge below.

§30.4 4. Two graves — mirages I killed before the skeptic

Grave #1 — "buy the tightest coil break" is a curve-fit onto one memory

The 07-01 coil was gorgeous, so I tried keying on compression alone: break of the prior range after atr_pct crushed below its daily median. Raising the volume bar or dropping it, the tight-coil-only rule barely fires 7× and nets +$11 at 57% (the TH=10/2.0× row) — a coin-flip. Most M2K range-pokes on a dead book snap straight back; that is why the faders earn here. Compression is a context, not a trigger.

Grave #2 — the tight stop gets shredded

M2K wiggles hard relative to its move size. A 6-pt stop with a 6-pt thrust (TH=6/2.0×/ST=6) drops win to 57% and net to +$84; the edge only holds when the stop is given ~8 pt of room (≥ the thrust size). The continuation is real but it breathes on the way — you must let it.

§30.5 5. The signal: Volume-Surge Thrust (VST)

Built on Family A's fingerprint, symmetric so it also shorts the roll-over continuations (#4). Fully mechanical on 5s bars, no discretion:

ComponentRule
ThrustNet move over trailing 5 min (300s) ≥ 6 pt in one direction
SurgeMean volume of the last 60s ≥ 1.8× the trailing 5-min baseline and ≥ 15 contracts/bar (kills the dead-tape false-fire)
ExtensionTrigger 5s bar closes in the thrust direction (green for longs / red for shorts)
EntryMarket at the trigger-bar close, in the thrust direction
Stop8 pt adverse (wide — ≥ the thrust; give it room)
ExitTime-stop at 15 min, else the 8-pt stop; 3-min cooldown between fires
Window / cost12:00–16:00 UTC; 0.4 pt ($2) round-trip charged (~1 tick slip + fees; spread is only ~0.17 pt)

§30.7 7. Verdict — a thin but genuine candidate

SHADOW This meets the letter of a new-edge candidate: it fires 18× (well beyond its 8 design moves), stays net-positive (+$274) after realistic costs, n ≥ 8, both sides pay, and it survives stripping its three best trades (+$93). It is a real, mechanical fingerprint — a volume surge out of a quiet coil — that the desk's faders structurally cannot see.

But I will not oversell it, because the skeptic is right to push:

  • Small money on a thin contract. +$274/week, +$15/trade. At the ×5 multiplier M2K's whole opportunity is small; this edge is real but it will never move the desk P&L much, and it is the contract most exposed to a slippage surprise on a fast bar.
  • One week, one regime mix. A momentum edge looks best in trending samples (07-02 carried it, 85% win); a fortnight of pure chop could flatten or flip it.
  • It only catches half the animals. The Family-B exhaustion reversals — which include the single biggest move (+$135) — have no generalising precursor in the captured data. Those fire on drying volume and are, honestly, either the faders' job or unpredictable thin-tape news. There is no greenfield signal for them, and I will not invent one.

Recommendation: run VST as a shadow on M2K in the 12:00–16:00 UTC window, log every fire, and demand it clears costs across a chop week before it earns a single live contract. It is a whisper, not a shout — but it is a genuine whisper.

◦ HONEST NULL — no net-positive rule this week
The lab found nothing that pays net of costs — meaning the desk's current refusal here is correct. A real null is a result, not a failure.

§31 Greenfield Signal Lab — MYM (Micro Dow)

MYM's eight biggest 15-minute moves this week are almost all up-runs off the US cash open — the exact directional legs the desk's faders structurally can't catch. The phenomenon is real and large (a naive “buy 13:35, sell 15:00” clock bet made +$471 and was green 5 days out of 5). But the precursor is a clock, not a microstructure tell: the 250 ms tape is silent before every launch, and every mechanical trigger I built only breaks even net of MYM's fat 2-point spread. Verdict: NO NEW EDGE — a real move with no extractable precursor on one bull week.

§31.1 1 · The eight biggest moves — they cluster at the open

Rolling 15-minute close-to-close scan of MYM 5s bars, whole week (Mon 06-29 → Fri 07-03 UTC), money at the 0.5 multiplier, de-overlapped:

#Start (UTC)Move$ (×0.5)Path
107-02 13:38:10+286 pt+$14352833→53119
206-29 13:37:15+269 pt+$13552406→52675
306-29 14:15:30+244 pt+$12252337→52581
407-01 13:38:45+239 pt+$12052401→52640
507-02 14:03:45+206 pt+$10352927→53133
606-30 13:37:20+205 pt+$10352417→52622
707-02 12:28:45+198 pt+$9952729→52927
807-01 14:01:35+187 pt+$9452592→52779

Two things jump out. Seven of the top eight are up-moves, and six of eight start between 12:28 and 14:16 UTC — the US equity morning (13:30 = 9:30 ET cash open). Most striking: four separate days launch within a minute of each other at 13:37–13:38 (06-29, 06-30, 07-01, 07-02) — roughly 7–8 minutes after the bell. That is a clock fingerprint, not a coincidence.

§31.2 2 · Precursor dissection — the open-flush-and-reclaim

Walking the 20–30 min before each launch at 5s-bar resolution shows one repeated shape. Per-minute volume before 13:30 idles at 100–450 contracts; at the 13:30 bell it detonates to 1,700–3,300 (5–10×). Price makes an early flush low in the first 6–8 minutes, then reclaims and grinds up for the rest of the morning. The 15-min windows in the table above all start at the bottom of that V (13:37–38) — the launch point.

MYM 06-29, 13:20–13:55 UTC — the open flush & reclaim. Blue = 5s price; grey = per-minute volume. Volume erupts at the 13:30 bell (dashed), price flushes to 52379, then reclaims through the open and runs to 52685.

The same V repeats every day: 06-30 flushed 52663→52383 then ran to 52622; 07-01 spiked 52650→52381 then ran to 52640; 07-02 dipped 52985→52823 then ran to 53133. Plain English: the open sells off hard on a burst of volume, prints a low inside the first eight minutes, then reclaims the opening level and trends up for the morning.

§31.3 3 · What the 250 ms tape says before the launch — nothing

Ticks (data/ticks.db) cover 07-01 to 07-03, so I could inspect the microstructure right before two of the 13:38 launches. This is the decisive test of whether there is a tradeable precursor versus just a clock. Aggregating quote_tick into 30s buckets around each launch:

Window07-01 spread07-01 book imb07-02 spread07-02 book imb
13:36:00–13:37:30 (pre)1.65–1.82 pt+0.00–+0.091.85–1.93 pt−0.05–+0.05
13:38:00–13:38:30 (launch)1.69–1.71 pt+0.01–−0.011.78–1.84 pt−0.01–−0.04
13:39:00–13:39:30 (running)1.72–1.76 pt−0.04–+0.121.73–1.77 pt−0.07–−0.01

The spread is flat at ~1.8 pt throughout — no compression, no widening. Book imbalance (bid_sz−ask_sz)/(bid_sz+ask_sz) sits on zero and never leans in the direction of the coming run — on 07-01 it is even mildly negative (sell-heavy) as price rips up, and quote arrival rate is a constant ~119/30s. We don't capture the aggressor tape, but the book and spread we do capture give zero forewarning. The launch is invisible at 250 ms. The only thing that predicts it is the wall clock.

§31.4 4 · The clock phenomenon is real — and only the clock

To size the effect cleanly, the crudest possible directional bet — buy MYM at 13:35, sell at 15:00, every day, no signal at all:

Day13:35→15:00$ net (cost $2)
06-29+30 pt+$13
06-30+198 pt+$97
07-01+377 pt+$187
07-02+223 pt+$110
07-03+134 pt+$65
Total+$471, 5/5 green
The catch

+$471 with a 100% day-win-rate looks like an edge, but it is n=5 and every single day was up. That is one directional bull week on the Dow, not a repeatable microstructure signal. A time-of-day long that only wins because the index rallied Monday-through-Friday will hand it all back the first down-open week. Five samples of “the market went up” is regime, not alpha.

§31.5 5 · Every mechanical trigger dies on the 2-point spread

I built four families of entry to try to convert the observed precursor into a rule that fires on more than the moves it was designed from, and simulated each across the whole week — every fire, winners and losers — net of a conservative $2 round-trip (MYM's 2-pt spread ≈ $1, plus ~$1 fees). Results:

Candidate entrynNet $Win%Read
Thrust-chase (buy the 3×-volume range-break bar)69−$9836%buys tops, whipsawed
VWAP-reclaim (cross back above session VWAP on volume)16−$9231%chopped at VWAP
Pullback to rising VWAP (buy the dip in trend)5+$1040%too rare (n<8)
Donchian continuation + trailing stop, 13:35–16:0020+$1545%breakeven / noise
  — same, first hour only (13:35–14:30)11+$145%breakeven / noise

The best generalising rule (Donchian breakout in the direction of a rising VWAP, trailing 30-pt stop) fires 20×, catches 6 of the 10 target big moves, and still nets only +$15 for the week. Gross of costs it is +$55; the $2×20 = $40 of spread-and-fees eats 73% of it. Average +$0.75 per trade — less than one tick, well inside the noise. The cumulative curve wanders around zero and closes where it started:

Best generalising signal — cumulative $ P&L, 20 fires across the week. Dashed line = breakeven. It drifts to −$21, claws back to +$37, ends +$15. That is a coin toss, not an edge.

§31.6 6 · Verdict

NO NEW-EDGE CANDIDATE  honest null

MYM's biggest moves this week are genuine directional runs off the US cash open that the desk's fader gates cannot catch — but they carry no extractable precursor:

  • The tell is a clock, not a signal. The 250 ms tape (spread flat ~1.8 pt, book imbalance ≈ 0, quote rate constant) is silent before every launch. Nothing in the captured microstructure or the funnel forewarns the move — only “it's ~8 minutes after the 13:30 bell” does.
  • Every generalising mechanical trigger breaks even at best. Chase and reclaim both lose (31–36% win, whipsawed); trend-continuation nets +$0.75/trade — under half MYM's spread. MYM's fat 2-point spread on a 0.5-multiplier contract is the structural killer (consistent with the desk's standing note that MYM is thin).
  • The one big number (+$471 clock bet) is n=5 on an all-up week — a single bull regime, indistinguishable from riding a rally, not a proven repeatable edge.

Do not ship a signal here. If anything is worth a look, it is a regime-gated morning-trend long (only enabled on days that open with the flush-reclaim shape), carried as an observe-only shadow across many more weeks to see whether the clock effect survives a down-open week — but on one week of data, with no microstructure precursor and no rule that clears the spread, there is nothing here that beats the noise. A real null beats a fake edge.

◦ HONEST NULL — no net-positive rule this week
The lab found nothing that pays net of costs — meaning the desk's current refusal here is correct. A real null is a result, not a failure.

§32 Greenfield Signal Lab — MCL: the coil is real, but crude fades the break

Every gate on this desk is a fader, so the money it structurally cannot touch is the directional run that keeps going. MCL is the desk's most event-driven micro (2× the equity-micro vol, OPEC/EIA-driven), so if any contract's big moves are catchable, it should be this one. I pulled the eight fattest 15-minute moves of the week, dissected the 20–30 minutes before each at 5s, 250 ms and funnel resolution, then built the obvious signal and simulated every fire across the week net of costs. There is genuinely good news and bad news. Good: the biggest moves share a real, visible precursor — a volume-dry range coil (8 of 10 dried, 6 of 10 compressed to a tight coil). Bad: that precursor is everywhere, and crude reverts the break. A trailing-range breakout on 1.5× volume has a negative forward mean (−0.02 pt, 41% win over n=1,131) and past-return→future-return correlation is −0.06 — momentum works against you here. Every breakout signal I built bleeds monotonically to −$1,238. This is why the faders earn their keep on MCL. Real null > fake edge — I am killing it before the skeptic does.

precursor is real, the trade is not — verdict

§32.1 1. The eight moves we missed

Top eight non-overlapping 15-minute MCL moves this week, close-to-close on 5-second bars, money at the ×100 multiplier (crude tick = 0.01 = $1). Tick coverage (data/ticks.db, 250 ms) begins 07-01 11:00 UTC; the funnel (fut_signal_funnel, 62,120 rows) covers the whole week. MCL traded zero live this week (observe-only), so this is pure greenfield.

#Start (UTC)Move$ (×100)SessionPrecursor shape
106-29 09:58−0.63−$63Londoncoil ($37→$19), vol drying
206-30 13:00+0.62+$62US pre-opencoil ($27→$10), vol drying
307-01 08:16−0.61−$61Londoncoil ($26→$10), vol drying
407-01 12:50+0.58+$58US pre-opensweep low → reverse up, vol build
506-29 06:17+0.58+$58London opencoil ($35→$18), vol drying
606-30 08:00+0.57+$57Londonvol build 2.6× rvol, break up
707-01 14:40−0.57−$57US morning (EIA day)slope down, vol drying

Two things stand out. First, the moves are balanced (4 up, 4 down) — there is no directional bias to lean on. Second, the clock is a little loud: six of the eight land in the 06:00–09:00 UTC London-crude ramp or the 12:50–14:40 US pre-open/EIA window (07-01 was an EIA Wednesday, and it printed three of the eight). But a time gate alone catches nothing tradeable, as section 5 shows.

§32.2 2. Dissecting the precursor — a volume-dry coil, then expansion

Slicing the 20 minutes before each move on 5s bars + the funnel gives a strikingly consistent fingerprint — and unlike gold's stealth grinds, MCL's is visible:

  • Volume dries into the move. In 8 of the 10 candidates, the last 5 minutes before the break ran on lower volume/min than the prior 15. Example: 07-01 08:47 (−$55) fell from 115 → 56 lots/min; 06-29 06:17 (+$58) from 48 → 36. Participation goes to sleep, then the range snaps.
  • Range compresses to a coil. In 6 of 10, the last-5-min high-low shrank below 60% of the prior 15-min range: 06-29 09:58 $37→$19; 07-01 08:47 $36→$11; 06-30 13:00 $27→$10. This is a textbook volatility-contraction→expansion coil.
  • Microstructure (250 ms) is quiet, not screaming. Around the 07-01 12:50 up-break the spread held a flat 2 ticks (2c) both before and during — no widening tell — and book imbalance in the final 60s was only +0.15 (a mild bid lean). The book does not telegraph direction ahead of time.
  • Funnel slope points the wrong way as often as not. On the down-moves vwap_slope_atr was negative (good), but on 06-29 09:58 (a −$63 down move) it read +1.39 and net_atr +2.16 — pointing up right before crude dropped. The slope is a coincident, not leading, read.

The precursor, drawn: 07-01 12:50 — coil, sweep the low, then break up

Blue = 5s close; grey = per-bar volume; shaded = the 20-min coil (~$17 range); green dashed = the break. Read it left-to-right: price grinds sideways in a tight band on a low, drying volume floor, dips to sweep the range low at 68.65 (a classic stop-run that looks like a down-break) — then reverses and lifts +$58. That head-fake is the whole problem: the coil is real, but which way it breaks (and whether the first break is the fake) is a coin-flip.

§32.3 3. The common signature — and why it defeats a directional signal

The up-runs and down-runs do share a setup (the volume-dry coil), which is more than gold offered. But grouping them exposes the trap: the coil predicts that a move is coming, not which way, and crude systematically fades the first break. The base rates are damning:

Base-rate test (whole week, 5s bars)nfwd 15-minwin%
Bar closes beyond trailing-5min high/low on ≥1.5× vol1,131mean −0.024, median −0.020 pt41%
Correlation of past-5min return → forward-15min return25,768r = −0.063 (mean-reverting)

A crude breakout wins only 41% of the time with a negative mean and negative median — the poke past the range reverts more often than it runs. And the past→future correlation is negative: at the 5s/15-min scale MCL is a mean-reverting instrument, so a continuation trade is fighting the tape. This is the mathematical reason the desk's faders exist and earn on MCL, and the reason a directional break-rider cannot.

§32.4 4. The signal I built anyway — Coil-Break with Volume Expansion

To prove it mechanically rather than by assertion, I built the best version of the precursor and ran it symmetric (long the up-break, short the down-break), on 5s bars, net of a realistic $3 round-trip (~2 ticks slippage + fees):

ComponentRule
Coil detecttrailing 5-min (60 bars) high-low range ≤ $20 (≤20 ticks)
Volume gatebreak bar volume ≥ 2× the coil's average per-bar volume
Confirm2 consecutive 5s closes beyond the coil edge (no chasing a single poke)
Entryat the confirming close, in the break direction
Stop / target1× coil range against / 4× coil range for, 15-min max hold

§32.5 5. Simulated across the whole week — every fire

It catches the moves and loses money doing it. The signal fired 279 times and hit 10 of the 10 target big moves — the precursor genuinely generalises — but it is drowned by ~190 fakeouts that revert straight into the stop.

§32.6 6. Verdict

L

NULL — the precursor is real, the edge is not. MCL's biggest moves are preceded by a visible volume-dry coil (8/10) that a signal catches 10/10 — so this is not the "unpredictable news" null. It is worse for a trend-rider: crude mean-reverts at this scale (breakout forward mean −0.02 pt, 41% win, r=−0.06), so standing in front of the run means eating a 59% wall of fakeouts that snap back. No parameterisation crosses zero. The honest read: the coil precursor belongs to the faders, not a new directional gate — the desk's existing reversion edge (quiet-tape, atr_pct≤0.07) is the correct way to monetise these coils, and MCL should stay observe-only until the crude spread is worth crossing. §33 Greenfield Signal Lab — MBT: the big moves are overshoots that snap back, not runs

The desk's gates are all faders, so the money they structurally cannot touch should be the directional run that keeps going. I went looking for that run in MBT (micro-Bitcoin, ×0.1) and did not find it. I pulled the eight fattest 15-minute moves of the week, dissected the 20–30 min before each at 5s, 250 ms and funnel resolution, then simulated every fire of the obvious signals across the whole week. The headline: MBT does not trend at this scale — it reverts. A momentum breakout loses catastrophically (−$3,226 over n=1,307, 29% win); past→future return correlation is negative (r=−0.10 at the 1-min→5-min horizon). The biggest moves are not runs at all — they are violent overshoots on a paper-thin book that immediately snap back (the top two moves, +$165 then −$145, are the same spike and its collapse). The tradeable edge is therefore the opposite of the mission's hypothesis: fade the thrust. That signal is net-positive even after paying the full (brutal) MBT spread — +$180/wk over n=97, 56% win — and it generalises well beyond its design moves. A real, if spread-constrained, new-edge candidate.

+$165 / −$145
top 2 moves = one 21:18 spike & its snap-back
r = −0.10
past-1m → fwd-5m return: MBT mean-reverts
29% / −$3,226
momentum breakout win% & net — the run is a mirage

spike-FADE net/wk, n=97, full-spread cost — candidate

§33.1 1. The eight moves we missed

Top eight non-overlapping 15-minute MBT moves this week, close-to-close on 5-second bars, money at the ×0.1 multiplier (MBT tick = 5 pt = $0.50). Tick coverage (data/ticks.db, 250 ms) begins 07-01 11:00 UTC; the funnel (fut_signal_funnel, 272,041 rows) covers the whole week. MBT is observe-only (traded zero live this week), so this is pure greenfield.

#Start (UTC)Move (pt)$ (×0.1)SessionPrecursor shape
106-29 21:18+1,655+$165overnight (thin)dead tape, ~0 vol → spike
206-29 21:33−1,450−$145overnight (thin)snap-back of #1

Two facts jump out. First, the moves come in reversing pairs: #1 and #2 are a single 06-29 21:18 up-spike (+$165) and the collapse right back down (−$145) fifteen minutes later; #4 and #6 are the identical shape on 07-01. These are not runs — they are overshoots. Second, the clock is loud: five of the eight land in the 21:00–22:00 UTC overnight window (US close, crypto still open but the futures book goes to sleep) or the 12:00–14:00 UTC US-data window.

§33.2 2. Dissecting the precursor — a thin book, then a thrust that overshoots

MBT is a paper-thin instrument, and that is the whole story. Across the week 70% of 5s bars printed zero volume (54,500 of 77,752), the average quoted spread was 23 pt (~$2.30 a side), and in the overnight spike windows the book was far worse. At 07-01 21:25–21:35 (around move #4) the 250 ms tape showed an average spread of 121.7 pt (~$12 a side) with only ~2 lots on each side of the book. When a market order hits a book that thin, price gaps — and then reverts once the imbalance clears. The fingerprint:

  • The book goes to sleep, then a single thrust overshoots. Before move #1 the tape was pinned in a 60400–60560 band on essentially zero volume (8→0.8 lots/min), then one thrust launched it +$169 in minutes. Thin liquidity is the accelerant, not a trend.
  • The overshoot mean-reverts. Move #2 (−$145) is literally move #1 unwinding. Whole-week base rate: a bar closing beyond its trailing-5min range on ≥2× volume has a forward-15min mean that is negative, and past→future return correlation is r = −0.10 (1m→5m) / −0.02 (5m→15m). MBT is a reverting instrument.
  • Several big moves are flush-and-reverse. #3 (+$114) and #7 (+$82) were both preceded by a sharp down-flush in the last five minutes (−260 pt, −180 pt) that then snapped up — a stop-run low swept and rejected.
  • The funnel confirms the regime split. Around the overnight spikes rvol read ~0.2 (dead tape) with a 78 pt funnel spread; the daytime move #3 read rvol 12.8 with a 13 pt spread. The overshoot-and-revert lives in the thin tape.

The precursor, drawn: 06-29 21:18 — dead tape, thrust, overshoot, snap-back

Blue = 5s close; grey = per-bar volume; shaded = the dead-tape coil; red dashed = the thrust trigger (+$17 in 60s). Read it left-to-right: price is pinned in a ~$16 band on a near-zero volume floor for twenty minutes, then a thrust rips it to 62090 (+$169) on a book with nothing in it — and immediately collapses −$145. The tradeable event is not the run up; it is the fade of the overshoot at the top.

§33.3 3. The common signature — and why it kills the directional thesis

Grouping the eight moves, the up-thrusts and down-thrusts share one setup: a violent thrust on a thin book that overshoots and reverses. There is no persistent run to ride — and the base rates prove a continuation trade is on the wrong side of the tape:

Base-rate test (whole week, 5s bars)nfwd returnwin%
Bar closes beyond trailing-5min range on ≥2× vol → continuation1,307net −$3,226, avg −$2.4729%
Same trigger, faded (enter opposite)1,410better, but tgt/stop-shaped losses36%
Correlation past-1min → forward-5min return94,205r = −0.100 (mean-reverting)

Momentum continuation wins only 29% of the time with a deeply negative mean — the poke past the range reverts far more often than it runs. This is the mathematical reason a directional break-rider cannot work on MBT, and why the correct trade is to fade the thrust with a reversion-shaped payoff (take profit on the snap-back, not chase the extension).

§33.4 4. The signal I built — Thin-Book Spike-Fade

Keyed on the precursor: when a fast thrust overshoots a thin book, sell it (or buy the down-flush) and target the snap-back. Symmetric, on 5s bars, one position at a time:

ComponentRule
Thrust detectclose moves ≥ 200 pt (~$20) in the trailing 60 s (12 bars)
Active tape≥1 lot traded in the thrust window (ignore pure quote-drift)
Entryat the thrust close, opposite the thrust (fade)
Target / stop1× the thrust size for (the snap-back) / 1× against; 15-min max hold
Refinementoptional rvol ≤ 1 gate — restrict to the thin tape where the overshoot is largest

§33.5 5. Simulated across the whole week — every fire

Costs are the whole ballgame on MBT, so I charged the real quoted spread at each trigger (crossing the full spread round-trip plus $0.90 fees — averaging $7.22/trade, and up to ~$10 on the overnight spikes), not a token tick. It still clears:

Variantnnet $win%avg $big-move hits
Spike-fade, nominal cost ($2 rt)97+$68763%+$7.085 / 8
Spike-fade, real full-spread cost (~$7/tr)97+$18056%+$1.865 / 8
  — overnight/other only60+$297—+$4.95—
  — US-day 12–17h only37−$117—−$3.16—
Spike-fade + rvol≤0.5 gate (thin-tape only)63+$21959%+$3.47—
Momentum breakout (continuation) — the mission's hypothesis1,307−$3,22629%−$2.47—

The fade fired 97 times — twelve times more than the eight moves it was designed on — hit 5 of the 8 targets, and stayed net-positive across a cost stress from $1.30 up to $4.50 round-trip (+$755→+$444). Crucially the mechanism is clean: filtering to the thin tape (rvol≤0.5) raises avg/trade from $1.86 to $3.47, exactly as the overshoot theory predicts, while the liquid US-day session is a bleed (−$117). The equity curve is a steady climb, not one lucky spike:

§33.6 6. Verdict

!

NEW-EDGE CANDIDATE — for shadow, with eyes open. The mission's directional-run thesis is falsified on MBT: momentum continuation loses −$3,226 (29% win) and the tape mean-reverts (r=−0.10). But the inverse signal is real. SHADOW Thin-Book Spike-Fade: fade a ≥200 pt/60s thrust, target the 1× snap-back, 15-min hold. It generalises (n=97, 12× its design moves), is net-positive even after paying the full MBT spread (+$180/wk, 56% win), and is mechanistically distinct from the desk's quiet-tape faders — it fires on violent thrusts in thin tape, not calm drift.

The honest caveats the skeptic will (rightly) press: (1) profit concentrates in the overnight 21:00 UTC thin-book spikes — only two such evenings drove the fat tail; (2) MBT's spread is so wide (~$7–12 round-trip) that a small mis-fill flips a winner; (3) the daytime session is a net loss, so this is a thin-tape signal, not an all-hours one. Recommendation: shadow it with the rvol≤1 gate and passive-limit entries before risking a cent live. Real edge, small and spread-constrained — but real.

Passive Limit Orders — Where They Work

Claim doesn't hold as stated: the +$153.92/34-fill headline (claimed +122) is inflated by variant double-counting (passive_chop_calm/_gated re-book the identical MNQ signal); the honest de-duplicated real figure is +$89.25 net over 25 fills (USD, already net of costs), and it is NOT propped up by dropping toxic fills (the lone no-fill was a missed winner) - it's a thin single-hour-of-22 multiple-comparison artefact on one week/4 days, neighbours and the full-week base strategy (-$2732/544 fills) deeply negative. Keep WATCH-ONLY, do not arm.

§34 Passive Limit-Order Lab — MNQ: the fills you get are the ones you didn't want

passive_chop rests a limit order and waits for price to come to it. All week on MNQ it looked like a money machine on paper — and lost real money in the tape. The reason is not fees or slippage in the ordinary sense; it is adverse selection. A resting limit only fills when price trades through your level — i.e. when you were wrong and it kept going. The times price would have bounced back to pay you, it never reached your limit: those are no-fills. So the strategy systematically keeps the losers and misses the winners. On MNQ this week the frictionless ledger read +$2,385 on the trades that filled; the realistic ledger on those exact same trades read −$3,298. That is not an edge with a haircut — it is a mirage with the wrong sign. One thin pocket (the 11:00 UTC London lull) survives on real fills, but it is a single hour out of twenty-two and I treat it as a watch, not a green light. Verdict: passive is not viable for MNQ — stay aggressive or don't trade.

693 / 74
passive fills vs no-fills (MNQ, this week)
+$2,385
frictionless P&L on the filled trades
−$3,298
REAL P&L on the same filled trades
−$5,683
the gap — pure adverse selection + spread

Scope: all three passive limit strategies on MNQ — passive_chop (596 signals), passive_chop_calm (149), passive_chop_gated (22) — re-priced trade-by-trade against the 250 ms quote tape in shadow_real.db. Trading week 2026-06-29 → 07-03 UTC. All figures below are the passive entry model (entry_model='passive'); money at the MNQ ×2 multiplier.

§34.1 1. The gap: paper says win, tape says bleed

Split MNQ's passive record into what filled and what didn't, frictionless (ceiling_pnl) vs realistic (real_pnl):

BucketnFrictionless (ceiling)Realistic (real)Gap
Filled — you entered693+$2,384.9−$3,297.7−$5,682.6
No-fill — never entered74+$1,205.1—unreachable
Whole universe of signals767+$3,590.0−$3,297.7−$6,887.7

Read it plainly. If you could magically fill every signal at the bar price, passive_chop would have booked +$3,590 on MNQ this week — the number that makes the strategy look alive. But a resting limit does not get that. Of the 767 signals, only 693 filled, and they filled precisely because price ran through the level. Re-priced against the real quote tape those 693 lose −$3,298. The 74 signals that would have been worth +$1,205 never filled — price bounced away from your limit before touching it. The average friction on a filled trade is $8.20, against a live MNQ spread of just 0.43 pt ($0.86). The extra $7-plus per trade is not the spread; it is the fills arriving at the worst possible moment.

The gap, drawn

Green = frictionless P&L on the filled trades. Red = the same trades re-priced on the real tape. Grey = the +$1,205 of bounces that never filled (unreachable). The sign flips.

§34.2 2. Adverse selection, dissected

Break the 693 filled trades by how they ended. This is the whole story in one table — the fills that trade through and keep going hit the stop; the ones that revert bank on the ratchet:

ExitnWinsAvg frictionlessAvg REALSum REAL
RATCHET (reverted — the bounce paid)439417+$13.85+$9.39+$4,121.3
STOP (traded through & kept going)2371−$14.75−$30.45−$7,217.7
FLAT_CLOCK (stale-out)170−$11.57−$11.84−$201.3

Look at the two averages on each row. On a winner, friction shaves $4.46 off (frictionless +$13.85 → real +$9.39) — you cross the spread on the way out and give a little back. On a loser, friction nearly doubles the damage ($14.75 → $30.45): the frictionless model assumes you filled at the bar, but the real fill happened as price sliced through your resting order and carried on. The loser is penalised 3.5× harder than the winner. That asymmetry — small haircut on the reverts, gaping wound on the through-fills — is adverse selection. Frictionlessly the 439 reverts (+$6,080) just out-earn the 237 through-fills (−$3,496), netting +$2,385. Realistically the through-fills deepen to −$7,218 and swamp everything.

And the winners you never got. The 74 no-fills carried +$1,205 of frictionless profit. Those are not random — a no-fill happens when price moves away in your favour without ever touching your limit, i.e. the clean bounce. You are structurally selected out of the best reverts and into the worst continuations. That +$1,205 is genuinely unreachable by a resting order at this level: to capture it you would have had to be aggressive and pay up front, which is a different strategy.

Where the real P&L comes from vs where it goes

Green = the reverts that paid (RATCHET). Red = the through-fills that ran to the stop. The red bar is the strategy.

§34.3 3. Fertile hunt — one thin pocket, twenty-one bleeds

I sliced the 693 fills every way the data allows and asked: is there any condition where MNQ passive is net-positive on real P&L with n≥15? Every volatility band, every volume band, every slope band is negative:

CutBandnSum REALWin%
entry_rvol<0.6 (quiet)325−$1,06062%
0.6–1.0249−$1,86156%
≥1.0 (busy)119−$37666%
atr_pct<0.045 (calm)473−$1,87657%
≥0.045 (livelier)220−$1,42264%
|vwap_slope|<0.7 (flat)300−$1,65662%
≥0.7 (sloped)393−$1,64259%

Notice the trap in the win-rate column: passive wins 55–66% of the time in every bucket and still loses money everywhere. That is the adverse-selection signature — lots of small reverts, a minority of fat through-fills. Raising the win rate does nothing because the loser is 3.5× the winner. No volatility or slope condition rescues it.

The only positive slice in the entire hunt is a clock cell: the 11:00–12:00 UTC hour (London lunch lull, ahead of the US cash session):

The one survivor — 11:00 UTC, and why I still won't trust it yet

PASSIVE fertile when hour = 11:00 UTC → real +$153.9 over 34 fills, win 82% (28 reverts, only 6 through-fills). Crucially there was just 1 no-fill in this hour (ceiling +$9.5), so there are no hidden missed winners flattering the number — it is clean on survivorship. Spread across the week it is 3 of 4 days positive (06-30 +$33, 07-02 +$145, 07-03 +$32; only 07-01 −$55). The mechanism is at least plausible: the pre-US lull is the quietest reversion tape of the day, so price pokes a level and snaps back rather than running — exactly when a resting limit is not adversely selected.

But: this is one hour out of twenty-two tested, and both neighbours bleed (10:00 UTC −$220, 12:00 UTC −$198). A lone positive cell flanked by losses is the classic shape of a multiple-comparisons artefact, not a mechanism. n=34 over a single week is thin. I log it as a watch — re-confirm it survives another 1–2 weeks of fills before it earns a shadow slot. It does not license turning MNQ passive on.

§34.4 4. The knob: does resting deeper help?

The honest limit: the re-price ran a single passive model — knob_offset = 0.0 for all 767 MNQ rows (rest at touch, knob_through = 1 whenever it filled). So I cannot empirically A/B a deeper or shallower offset from this table; the data does not contain the variants. What it does tell you, mechanically, is decisive: resting deeper (further from touch) only fills when price has travelled even further against you — it strictly worsens the through-fill toxicity while converting more of the +$1,205 bounces into no-fills. Resting shallower converges on the aggressive model, which pays the spread up front. The two aggressive comparators that were re-priced this week both also bled on MNQ (dip_loose −$74.5 / 25 fills; pullback_loose −$112.3 / 32 fills), but far less per trade than passive's through-fills. There is no offset on the toxic side of the trade that skips the loser and keeps the winner — that is the definition of adverse selection, and no knob undoes it. To add a real knob sweep, the re-pricer needs to emit multiple knob_offset variants; flagged for the pipeline.

§34.5 5. Verdict

Passive limit orders are a frictionless mirage for MNQ. The +$3,590 paper number is unreachable: a resting order fills you into the 237 continuations (−$7,218 real) and locks you out of the cleanest 74 bounces (+$1,205 you never touch). On real fills the strategy loses −$3,298 this week, and no volatility, volume or slope condition turns it green. The single positive cell — the 11:00 UTC lull, +$154 over 34 fills at 82% — is clean on survivorship but statistically isolated and one week old; it is a watch candidate, not a shadow arm. For live money the read is unambiguous: do not run passive_chop on MNQ — be the aggressor that pays the 0.43 pt spread, or stand aside.

Sources: shadow_real.db shadow_real_pnl (real re-price), data/alphabot.db fut_shadow_sim_trades (conditions), data/ticks.db quote_tick (spread). MNQ passive, week 2026-06-29→07-03 UTC, ×2 multiplier. All P&L in USD.

◦ HONEST NULL — no net-positive rule this week
The lab found nothing that pays net of costs — meaning the desk's current refusal here is correct. A real null is a result, not a failure.

§35 MES Passive Limit-Order Lab — the frictionless mirage, dissected

The passive_chop family rests a limit order in the chop and waits to be filled. All week it ran in shadow, and every fill was re-priced under a realistic model that only fills you when price actually trades through your level. This lab asks one question for MES: once you strip the fantasy fills, is there any condition where resting a limit makes money — or is adverse selection simply fatal? The passive strategies overlap heavily (passive_chop_calm and passive_chop_gated are subsets that re-log the same signal), so every number below is deduplicated to the passive_chop superset: one row per distinct entry (timestamp×side). Money is USD at the $5 MES multiplier; the £ verdict figure is converted at 0.79.

Verdict up front

Passive on MES is a mirage that isn't even shiny. Friction-free, the filled set is already −$661 and the whole book (fills + missed bounces) nets roughly −$84 — there was never a fat frictionless edge here to lose. Re-priced on realistic through-fills, the reachable trades collapse to −$1,616 over 303 fills, while the +$578 of genuine bounces sit in the 143 no-fills you could never reach. That is textbook adverse selection: you fill the losers and miss the winners. Swept across rvol, volatility, VWAP-slope, time-of-day, regime and side, no condition produces a net-positive real_pnl pocket at n ≥ 15. The one positive time bucket (00:00 UTC) is a single session that shrinks to n=10 on dedup. No shadow candidate. Stay aggressive or don't trade MES passively. £0/wk.

§35.1 1 · The gap — frictionless vs. real, and the fills you never got

MES rested 446 distinct passive signals this week. 303 filled; 143 never traded through and so never entered. The chart shows the three numbers that matter: what the fills are actually worth (real), what the same fills would be worth with free fills (frictionless ceiling), and the ceiling P&L of the bounces you missed.

SetnReal P&LFrictionless (ceiling)Gap
Filled (reachable)303−$1,616−$661−$955
No-fill (unreachable)143—+$578—
Whole book, frictionless446—−$84—

Two things jump out. First, the −$955 gap on the fills alone is pure fill friction: −$3.15 per fill. MES quotes averaged a 1.12-tick spread (~$1.40) over 1.28M quote ticks this week, so $3.15 is a full spread plus the adverse drift you inherit by only filling when price is running through you. Second — and this is the honest kicker — the frictionless whole-book number is only −$84. The desk-wide "+$10k frictionless" story is other contracts; on MES there was no juicy frictionless edge that friction ate. The edge was never there. What friction does is concentrate the entire loss into the half of the book you can actually reach.

§35.2 2 · Adverse selection, dissected

A resting limit is a bet that price touches your level and reverts. It fills only when price trades through — which is exactly when you were wrong and it kept going. Every single one of MES's 303 fills was a through-fill (knob_through=1 for 100% of them). So the fills are structurally skewed to the trades that ran against you, and the reverts that would have paid you are disproportionately in the no-fill pile.

The split is the whole argument. Of the fills you got, 64% (194/303) were losers on real P&L. Of the fills you missed, 57% (82/143) were winners on ceiling. You are systematically keeping the wrong half. The exit ledger says the same thing another way:

Exit reason (filled)nReal P&LRead
STOP192−$2,677through-fills that kept going → 1-ATR stop
RATCHET98+$908the ones that did revert — banked small
INCOMPLETE10+$133still open at cutoff
FLAT_CLOCK3+$19timed out flat

The reverts that fill do ratchet a profit — but at +$9.81 average they are smaller than the through-fill losses at −$13.84 average, and there are half as many of them. The winners are muted (you got a bad entry even when right) and the losers run. Unreachable edge: the 82 missed winners were worth +$1,168 of ceiling; the 61 losers you dodged saved only −$591. The net bounce you can't touch is +$578, and it lives almost entirely in the no-fill pile by construction.

§35.3 3 · Fertile hunt — is there any condition that escapes it?

I swept the 303 real fills across every conditioning column, hunting a net-positive real_pnl pocket with n ≥ 15. Representative slices (all deduplicated):

ConditionnReal P&LWin%
rvol < 0.15 (deadest tape)18−$13811%
rvol 0.3–0.6142−$89530%
atr_pct < 0.07 (calm)296−$1,57336%
|vwap_slope| < 0.02 (flat)7−$960%
router_regime = INDEX433*−$2,32036%
SHORT side232*−$1,47532%
atr_pct ≥ 0.07 (busy)7−$4357%
entry hour 00:00 UTC10+$6150%

*regime/side rows shown pre-dedup for direction; every band is negative either way.

Nothing clears the bar. The only positive bucket is entry hour 00:00 UTC (8pm New York, evening Globex), and it fails on two counts: deduplicated it is n=10, below the n≥15 floor, and it is a single session — 07-02 lost −$79 and 07-03 made +$201, i.e. five ratchet winners on one quiet night. That is a coin, not a condition. The calm-tape thesis (which is MES's real reversion edge on aggressive entries) makes passive worse, not better: atr_pct<0.07 is where the bulk of the −$1,573 loss sits, because in dead tape your limit only fills when a lone sweep runs through it. There is no rvol×vol×slope corner where the through-fill toxicity flips positive.

§35.4 4 · The knob — would resting deeper help?

Honest data limitation: for MES the shadow logged a single knob_offset (0.0) and knob_through=1 on every fill, so I cannot empirically test resting the limit deeper or shallower from this week's record. But the mechanism is not ambiguous. Resting deeper (further from the mid) fills you even less often — and the fills you do get are, if anything, further through the level, i.e. more toxic. It shrinks the already-negative reachable set toward the worst tail. Resting shallower just converges on the aggressive/through model, which is the correct model for MES: cross the spread, take the fill you can trust, and let the calm-tape reversion edge (documented elsewhere in this report) do the work. There is no offset that carves out a clean pocket of touch-and-revert fills, because 100% of the passive fills this week were through-fills to begin with. The knob cannot rescue what the fill mechanism poisons.

Bottom line for MES

Passive limit entry on MES is a frictionless mirage — and a thin one. The friction-free book is only −$84 to start with, so there was no edge to lose; realistic through-fills then dump the entire loss onto the half you can reach (−$1,616 / 303 fills) while parking +$578 of real bounces in the no-fills you never get. Adverse selection is structural, not a threshold you can tune around: every fill traded through, the fills are 64% losers, the no-fills are 57% winners. No conditioning band survives at n≥15 net-positive; the lone positive time bucket is one session. No shadow candidate. £0/wk of honest passive money. MES's edge is aggressive calm-tape reversion — keep entering aggressive, and do not rest limits into MES chop.

✓ CANDIDATE HELD — £448/wk survived the skeptic (loaded into your Playbook)
Reproduces at +$569.16 USD net (=~£448 at 1.27, i.e. the claimed +450 was GBP) over 57 filled (n>=15 ✓); NOT a no-fill survivorship artifact (7 no-fills are missed winners at +$186 ceiling, filled sum stands alone net of costs) — but it IS thin scan-noise: ~94% of PnL from 2 of 4 sessions, mechanism-free, wedged beside the -$690 13:00 UTC hour → analyst's classification holds: WATCH-only, DO-NOT-ARM, retire if it fails to replicate.

§36 Passive Limit-Order Lab — MGC (gold micro)

The passive_chop family (chop, chop_calm, chop_gated) rested limit orders in gold's quiet tape in shadow all week (Mon 06-29 → Fri 07-03 UTC). On a frictionless mark those fills look like free money. This lab re-prices every one of them on the realistic fill model in shadow_real.db — a resting limit only fills when price trades through your level (you were wrong and it kept going); the times gold would have bounced back to pay you are recorded as no-fills. That asymmetry is adverse selection, and it is the whole story here. Money shown in £ at 0.79 GBP/USD; MGC = $10/point, tick 0.10 pt = $1.00.

HEADLINE

MGC passive is a frictionless mirage. The 653 filled passive limits marked +£1,004 frictionless but −£3,034 realistic — an adverse-selection tax of −£4,038 on the fills alone. On top of that, the 147 orders that never filled carried +£1,510 of ceiling: those were exactly the bounces that would have paid, and passive systematically missed them. So it keeps the losers and skips the winners — textbook. The only real-positive slices are single-hour scan artifacts (12:00 and 15:00 UTC) concentrated on one or two days with no mechanism — and the NY-open hour between them (13:00) is the single worst loser of the week. Verdict: adverse selection is fatal for MGC passive. Stay aggressive or don't trade. No arm.

§36.1 1) The gap — frictionless vs realistic

Every passive fill and no-fill for MGC this week, split by whether the limit actually filled. Frictionless (ceiling_pnl) is the fantasy; realistic (real_pnl) is what a resting order really banks. A no-fill banks £0 — you never held the position — so its ceiling is missed, not earned.

BucketnFrictionless (ceiling)Realistic (real)Gap
Filled653+£1,004−£3,034−£4,038
No-fill (missed)147+£1,510£0 (never held)−£1,510
Passive total800+£2,514 (fantasy)−£3,034 (real)−£5,548

Per strategy the bleed is uniform: passive_chop −£2,338 real / 450 fills, passive_chop_calm −£618 / 192, passive_chop_gated −£77 / 11. Not one passive variant is net-positive on realistic fills.

§36.2 2) Adverse selection, dissected

Every filled passive order this week had knob_through = 1 — by construction it filled only because price traded through the level. The question is what happened next. Of the 653 fills, 394 kept going (losers) and 259 reverted enough to pay (winners) — a 40% win rate. Worse, the losers are bigger: average win +$21.47, average loss −$23.86. Small edge, wrong tail, negative sum: +$5,561 of wins buried under −$9,401 of losses = −$3,840 (−£3,034).

The mirror image is the 147 no-fills carrying +£1,510 of ceiling. Those are gold ticks that approached the limit and bounced without trading through — the clean reverts, the trades a passive order most wants, and it caught none of them. So the unreachable edge is roughly £1,510 of missed winners on top of the £4,038 tax on the fills it did get. You cannot cherry-pick your way out: filtering the filled set to only its winners is survivorship fiction, because you would have had to hold the 394 losers to be resting for the 259 wins.

§36.3 3) Fertile hunt — is there any condition that escapes it?

I swept the filled passive set across every knob available: rvol band, ATR% (vol) band, |VWAP-slope| band, and hour-of-day. Every volatility, rvol and slope band is net-negative — there is no calm-tape carve-out that flips gold passive positive:

CutBest sub-bandnrealwin%Read
rvol<0.15 (deadest tape)19−$0.342%breakeven at best, all others worse
ATR%0.07–0.1077−$17851%least-bad, still red
|VWAP slope|<0.1 (flat)28−$6346%flat tape still bleeds

The only real-positive slices are two isolated hours of the UTC day — and they fail the smell test. 12:00 UTC (pre-NY-open) nets +$351 / 28 fills / 64% win and 15:00 UTC (NY mid-morning) nets +$219 / 29 fills / 69% win; combined that hour-pair is +$569 / 57 fills / 67% win (≈ +£450). But: (a) $281 of the 12:00 total is a single day (07-02), and $181 of the 15:00 total is a single day (07-01) — strip those and both collapse toward breakeven; (b) they were found by scanning all 24 hours, and the hour between them — 13:00 UTC, the NY cash open — is the week's worst at −$690 / 27 fills. A signal that flips from −$690 to +$351 to +$219 across three adjacent hours is noise, not structure. No credible mechanism separates 12:00 and 15:00 from 13:00.

FERTILE POCKET (thin — do not arm)

MGC passive fertile when hour-of-day ∈ {12:00, 15:00} UTC → real +£450 over 57 fills, 67% win. This clears the numeric bar (net-positive real, n ≥ 15, and its 7 no-fills carry only +$186 ceiling so it is not a survivorship artifact). But it is day-concentrated (two days carry it), mechanism-free, and sits either side of the worst hour of the week. Treat as a WATCH curiosity for continued shadow observation, not a shadow-candidate to arm. One more week that fails to replicate it should retire the idea.

§36.4 4) The knob — can a deeper limit skip the toxic fills?

The natural defence against adverse selection is to rest the limit deeper, so only a real overshoot fills you. Unfortunately the shadow only recorded one knob setting: knob_offset = 0.0 (at-touch) for all 800 MGC orders. There is no shallower/deeper variant in the data to compare, so this question is unanswerable from this week's capture — a capture gap, not a result. Given gold's spread averaged 0.229 pt (≈ 2.3 ticks, ≈ $2.29) with a fat tail to 9+ ticks, a deeper limit would fill even less often and hand still more of the reverts to the no-fill bucket — the prior is that deeper makes the fill/no-fill asymmetry worse, not better. To actually test it, the shadow needs to re-price MGC at several knob_offset levels next week.

§36.5 5) Verdict

Passive limits are not viable on MGC under any condition this week isolates. The frictionless +£1,004 is a mirage; realistically the fills bled −£3,034 and passive additionally skipped +£1,510 of the bounces that would have paid — a −£5,548 round-trip of adverse selection. Every volatility/rvol/slope carve-out stays red. The one positive slice (12:00 & 15:00 UTC) is a two-day, mechanism-free scan artifact wedged either side of the week's worst hour, and the deeper-limit escape hatch is untestable because only the at-touch knob was captured. For MGC: if you want in, do it aggressively — the through-fill strategies at least escape the no-fill asymmetry (pullback_loose was +$22 real / 69 fills, though dip_loose was −$146, so aggressive is no free lunch either) — or don't trade gold passively at all. Do not arm passive_chop on MGC.

◦ HONEST NULL — no net-positive rule this week
The lab found nothing that pays net of costs — meaning the desk's current refusal here is correct. A real null is a result, not a failure.

§37 M2K Passive Limit-Order Lab — adverse selection on the micro Russell

A resting limit is a bet that price touches your level and reverts. It fills only when price trades through — exactly when you were wrong and it kept going. Unreachable edge: the 11 missed winners were worth +$39.39 of ceiling, but the 13 losers you dodged saved −$37.82, so the net missed bounce is a trivial +$1.57. On M2K there isn't even a fat pile of missed winners to chase — the no-fills are a near-perfect wash. The damage is entirely the through-fills you do get.

§37.3 3 · Fertile hunt — is there any condition that escapes it?

I swept the 33 real fills across every conditioning column, hunting a net-positive real_pnl pocket at n ≥ 15. Representative slices (all deduplicated):

ConditionnReal P&LWin%
Whole filled book33−$76.2124%
rvol > 1 (busy tape)13−$45.0615%
rvol ≤ 0.6 (quiet tape)10−$12.2030%
|vwap_slope| ≤ 0.5 (flat)13−$19.5638%
|vwap_slope| > 1.5 (trending)10−$45.450%

Nothing clears the bar. Every band is negative, and — critically — the only slice that even reaches the n≥15 floor is the whole book, which loses −$76. The moment you condition down to a plausibly-fertile corner (quiet tape, flat slope), the sample collapses to n=10–13 and stays red anyway. The quiet-tape thesis that carries M2K's aggressive reversion edge makes passive less bad (−$12 on 10 fills) but not positive: in dead tape your limit only fills when a lone sweep runs through it, so you inherit the sweep. Trending tape (|slope|>1.5) is outright toxic — 0/10 winners, −$45. Two hourly buckets closed green (13:00 UTC +$4.65 n=5; 15:00 UTC +$10.76 n=2) but those are 5- and 2-trade coins on a single session, not conditions. There is no rvol×slope×time corner where the through-fill toxicity flips positive.

§37.4 4 · The knob — would resting deeper help?

Honest data limitation: the M2K shadow logged a single knob_offset (0.0) and knob_through=1 on every fill, so I cannot empirically test resting the limit deeper or shallower from this week's record. But the mechanism is not ambiguous. Resting deeper (further from mid) fills you even less often, and the fills you do get are further through the level — i.e. more toxic, shrinking the already-negative reachable set toward its worst tail. Resting shallower just converges on the aggressive/through model, which is the correct model for M2K: cross the ~1–2 tick spread, take a fill you can trust, and let the quiet-tape reversion edge do the work. There is no offset that carves out a clean pocket of touch-and-revert fills, because 100% of the passive fills this session were through-fills to begin with. The knob cannot rescue what the fill mechanism poisons.

Bottom line for M2K

Passive limit entry on M2K is a loser at the source, not just after friction. The friction-free book is only −$24 to start with — there was no edge to lose; realistic through-fills then dump the whole loss onto the half you can reach (−$76.21 / 33 fills, −£60) while the no-fills net a trivial +$1.57. Adverse selection is structural, not a threshold you can tune around: every fill traded through, 76% of fills lost, and no conditioning band survives at n≥15 net-positive — the only n≥15 slice is the whole book, and it bleeds. Caveat honestly held: this is one session of data. No shadow candidate. £0/wk of honest passive money. M2K's edge is aggressive quiet-tape reversion — keep entering aggressive, and do not rest limits into M2K chop.

◦ HONEST NULL — no net-positive rule this week
The lab found nothing that pays net of costs — meaning the desk's current refusal here is correct. A real null is a result, not a failure.

§38 MYM Passive Limit-Order Lab — adverse selection on the micro Dow

The passive_chop family rests a limit order in the chop and waits to be filled. It ran in shadow this week and every fill was re-priced under a realistic model that only fills you when price actually trades through your level — i.e. when you were wrong and it kept going. This lab asks one question for MYM: strip the fantasy fills, and is there any condition where resting a limit makes money, or is adverse selection simply fatal? passive_chop_calm re-logs the same signals as an exact subset (all 10 calm rows match a passive_chop entry at the same timestamp×side — 0 unmatched), so every number below is deduplicated to the passive_chop superset: one row per distinct entry. Money is USD at the $0.50 MYM multiplier; £ converts at 0.79. Health warning up front: the MYM shadow only fired on a single session (2026-07-03) — 41 signals, 23 fills. n is thin and every read here is one-day evidence.

Verdict up front

Passive on MYM is a textbook adverse-selection loss. The reachable fills are already −$36 frictionless and collapse to −$83.76 over 23 fills (−£66) once you price the through-fills honestly. Meanwhile the bounces you couldn't reach — the no-fills — carried +$95.65 of winning ceiling you never touched. You systematically keep the losers and miss the winners: 100% of fills were through-fills, 74% of them lost, and 16 of 23 stopped out for −$104.92. Swept across rvol, ATR, VWAP-slope, side and time-of-day, no condition yields a net-positive real_pnl pocket at n ≥ 15 — the only n≥15 slice is trending tape, and it loses −$66. No shadow candidate. Stay aggressive or don't rest limits into MYM chop. £0/wk.

§38.1 1 · The gap — frictionless vs. real, and the fills you never got

MYM rested 41 distinct passive signals this session. 23 filled; 18 never traded through and so never entered. The chart shows the three numbers that matter: what the fills are actually worth (real), what the same fills would be worth with free fills (frictionless ceiling), and the net ceiling P&L of the bounces you missed.

SetnReal P&LFrictionless (ceiling)Gap
Filled (reachable)23−$83.76−$36.02−$47.74
No-fill (unreachable)18—+$54.18—
Whole book, frictionless41—+$18.16—

Read it carefully, because this is the whole thesis in three numbers. Frictionless, the whole book looks like a modest winner (+$18.16) — that is the "+$10k frictionless" mirage in miniature. But the +$18 lives almost entirely in the no-fills (+$54.18 of ceiling you never reach) while the fills you actually get are already frictionless-negative (−$36.02). Then realism prices the through-fills: the reachable set drops to −$83.76 — a −$47.74 fill-friction gap, −$2.08 per fill. MYM quoted a ~2-point spread (median 2 pts, mean 1.91) over 231,471 quote ticks; at $0.50/point that is ~$1.00 one-way, so the per-fill loss is roughly a full round-trip of spread plus the adverse drift you inherit by only filling when price runs through you. The mirage isn't that friction ate a fat edge — it's that the "edge" was sitting in the half of the book you can never touch.

§38.2 2 · Adverse selection, dissected

A resting limit is a bet that price touches your level and reverts. It fills only when price trades through — exactly when you were wrong and it kept going. Every one of MYM's 23 fills was a through-fill (knob_through=1 for 100%). So the fills are structurally skewed to the trades that ran against you, and the reverts that would have paid you sit disproportionately in the no-fill pile.

The split is the argument. Of the fills you got, 74% (17/23) lost on real P&L — a 26% win rate. Of the fills you missed, 56% (10/18) were winners on ceiling, and those 10 missed winners were worth +$95.65 — a genuine fat pile of bounces you could never reach (the 8 missed losers you dodged saved only −$41.47, netting the +$54.18 above). You are keeping the wrong half of the book. The exit ledger says it a third time:

Exit reason (filled)nReal P&LCeilingRead
STOP16−$104.92−$99.47through-fills that kept going → 1-ATR stop
RATCHET6+$16.78+$62.57the ones that did revert — but the fill haircut cost 73% of the frictionless win
FLAT_CLOCK1+$4.38+$0.88timed out roughly flat

16 of 23 fills (70%) stopped out for −$104.92 — that is the adverse-selection engine in one line: you fill precisely when the move is running through you, then eat the 1-ATR stop. The 6 reverts that did fill ratcheted, but realism haircut them from +$62.57 of frictionless ceiling down to +$16.78 real — you got a worse entry even when the idea was right, keeping barely a quarter of the winner. Unreachable edge: add the +$95.65 of missed winners to the winner-haircut and the honest picture is that the profitable side of passive on MYM is structurally out of reach. The damage is the through-fills; the reward is in the no-fills. That is adverse selection, not a tuning problem.

§38.3 3 · Fertile hunt — is there any condition that escapes it?

I swept the 23 real fills across every conditioning column, hunting a net-positive real_pnl pocket at n ≥ 15. Representative slices (all deduplicated to passive_chop):

ConditionnReal P&LWin%
Whole filled book23−$83.7626%
|vwap_slope| > 1 (trending)15−$65.8020%
ATR ≥ 8 pts (high vol)11−$76.329%
rvol > 1 (busy tape)12−$35.1925%
ATR < 8 pts (quiet tape)12−$7.4442%
|vwap_slope| ≤ 1 (flat)8−$17.9638%
rvol ≤ 0.6 (quiet tape)5−$10.6040%

Nothing clears the bar. Every band is negative, and the only slice that even reaches the n≥15 floor is trending tape (|slope|>1), which loses −$65.80 at a 20% win rate — the worst kind of pocket, exactly where a limit is most likely to be run over. The quiet-tape thesis that carries MYM's aggressive reversion edge does make passive less bad (ATR<8: −$7.44 on 12 fills, 42% win; rvol≤0.6: −$10.60 on 5), but it never turns positive and it never reaches n≥15 — the moment you condition into the plausibly-fertile corner the sample collapses to 5–12 fills and stays red. High-vol (ATR≥8) is outright toxic: 1 win in 11, −$76.32. There is no rvol×slope×ATR×time corner where the through-fill toxicity flips net-positive at a defensible n.

§38.4 4 · The knob — would resting deeper or shallower help?

Honest data limitation: the MYM shadow logged a single knob_offset (0.0) and knob_through=1 on every fill, so I cannot empirically test resting the limit deeper or shallower from this week's record. But the mechanism is not ambiguous. Resting deeper (further from mid) fills you even less often, and the fills you do get are further through the level — more toxic, pushing the already-negative reachable set toward its worst tail (the −$105 stop pile). Resting shallower just converges on the aggressive/through model — which is the correct model for MYM: cross the ~2-point spread, take a fill you can trust, and let quiet-tape reversion do the work. There is no offset that carves out a clean pocket of touch-and-revert fills, because 100% of the passive fills this session were already through-fills. The knob cannot rescue what the fill mechanism poisons.

Bottom line for MYM

Passive limit entry on MYM is a frictionless mirage, not an edge. The whole book looks like +$18 free, but that money is entirely in no-fills you can't reach; the fills you actually get are −$36 frictionless and −$83.76 / 23 fills (−£66) real, while +$95.65 of winning bounces stay unreachable. Adverse selection is structural, not a threshold you can tune around: every fill traded through, 74% lost, 16 of 23 stopped out, and no conditioning band survives at n≥15 net-positive — the only n≥15 slice (trending tape) bleeds −$66. Caveat honestly held: this is a single session of MYM data, and the smallest-multiplier contract on the desk. No shadow candidate. £0/wk of honest passive money. MYM's edge is aggressive quiet-tape reversion — keep entering aggressive, and do not rest limits into MYM chop.

The lab found nothing that pays net of costs — meaning the desk's current refusal here is correct. A real null is a result, not a failure.

§39 Passive Limit-Order Lab — MCL (crude micro)

The passive_chop family rested limit orders in crude's tape in shadow and every fill was re-priced on the realistic fill model in shadow_real.db. A resting limit only fills when price trades through your level — you were wrong and it kept going; the times crude would have bounced back to pay you are recorded as no-fills. That asymmetry is adverse selection, and for MCL it is the whole story. Money in £ at 0.79 GBP/USD; MCL = $100/point, tick 0.01 pt = $1.00. Caveat up front: the realistic reprice for MCL covers a single UTC day (2026-07-03, 08:33→16:51) — 77 orders. Small sample; read the direction, not the decimal.

HEADLINE

MCL passive is worse than a mirage — it is a loser even frictionless. The full 153-order frictionless shadow marked −£87; there is no fantasy profit to erode. On the re-priced day the 41 filled limits marked −£68 frictionless but −£156 realistic — an adverse-selection tax of −£87 on the fills alone, on top of which the 36 orders that never filled carried the day's +£116 of missed-winner ceiling: those were exactly the bounces that would have paid. Keep the losers, skip the winners — textbook. Win rate on fills was 27%. Every volatility, rvol, slope and hour band is net-negative; the only positive slice (dead-quiet tape) is n=7, far below the bar. Verdict: adverse selection is fatal for MCL passive. Crude's event-driven vol and ~1.6-tick spread make it the wrong instrument to rest into. Stay observe-only or aggressive. No arm.

§39.1 1) The gap — frictionless vs realistic

Every re-priced passive order for MCL, split by whether the limit filled. Frictionless (ceiling_pnl) is the fantasy; realistic (real_pnl) is what a resting order really banks. A no-fill banks £0 — you never held it — so its ceiling is missed, not earned.

BucketnFrictionless (ceiling)Realistic (real)Gap
Filled41−£68−£156−£87
No-fill (missed)36+£25 net (+£116 winners / −£91 dodged)£0 (never held)−£25
Passive total (day)77−£44 (fantasy)−£156 (real)−£112

And the full-shadow frictionless mark is no rescue: all 153 passive_chop orders sum to −£87 frictionless (the lone passive_chop_calm order was +£12). MCL passive does not even clear its own fantasy — there is no ceiling edge to protect. This matches the standing read that MCL is 2× equity-micro vol, event-driven, and negative on every gate after spread.

§39.2 2) Adverse selection, dissected

All 41 fills had knob_through = 1 — by construction each filled only because price traded through the limit. What happened next is the whole edge. Of the 41 fills, 30 kept going (losers) and 11 reverted enough to pay (winners) — a 27% win rate. The tail is wrong too: average win +$3.93, average loss −$8.00. Small right tail, fat left tail: +$43 of wins buried under −$240 of losses = −$197 (−£156).

The mirror image is the selection itself. The trades that filled averaged −$2.11 ceiling; the trades that didn't averaged +$0.87 ceiling. Filling literally sorts crude into the losers. And the 36 no-fills contained 17 clean reverts worth +$146 of ceiling — the bounces that approached the limit and turned without trading through, exactly what a passive order most wants, and it caught none of them. So the unreachable edge is roughly £116 of missed winners on top of the £87 fill-price tax. You cannot cherry-pick out: to be resting for the 11 wins you had to hold all 30 losers.

§39.3 3) Fertile hunt — is there any condition that escapes it?

I swept the 41 filled orders across every knob available: side, rvol band, ATR (vol) band, |VWAP-slope| band, and time-of-day. Every single band is net-negative. There is no calm-tape, low-vol or flat-slope carve-out that flips MCL passive positive:

CutLeast-bad sub-bandnrealwin%Read
sideSHORT26−$12427%both sides red (LONG −$73 / 15)
rvol<0.5 (quiet)14−$4443%least-bad, still red, n<15
ATR0.05–0.06519−$8526%every vol band bleeds
|VWAP slope|<0.5 (flat)15−$5333%flat tape still bleeds
hourpm (≥14:00 UTC)13−$3838%best hour block, still red

The only real-positive slice is the intersection of the two quietest knobs — rvol < 0.5 AND |VWAP-slope| < 0.5, which nets +$6.16 over just 7 fills (5/7 win). It is directionally the right story (reverts live in dead tape, as they do across the desk), but at n=7 it fails the n ≥ 15 bar and cannot be promoted. Every band that does reach n ≥ 15 is red. There is no qualifying fertile pocket for MCL passive.

NO FERTILE POCKET

No condition rescues passive for MCL. The best n ≥ 15 slice is rvol < 0.5 at −$44 / 14…15 fills; the only positive carve-out (dead-quiet rvol & slope both <0.5) is +$6 on 7 fills and fails the sample bar. Adverse selection is fatal here. Treat the quiet-tape hint as a WATCH for continued shadow observation only — nothing to arm.

§39.4 4) The knob — can a deeper limit skip the toxic fills?

The natural defence against adverse selection is to rest the limit deeper, so only a real overshoot fills you. But the shadow recorded exactly one setting: knob_offset = 0.0 (at-touch) for all 77 MCL orders, and every fill flagged knob_through = 1. There is no shallower/deeper variant to compare, so this is unanswerable from this capture — a data gap, not a result. Prior: MCL's quote spread averaged 0.016 pt (≈ 1.6 ticks, ≈ $1.61) across 724k quotes, and crude is event-driven (OPEC / EIA-Wed-10:30 / geopolitics), so a deeper limit would fill even less often and hand still more of the reverts to the no-fill bucket. Deeper almost certainly makes the fill/no-fill asymmetry worse, not better. To actually test it, next week's shadow must re-price MCL at several knob_offset levels.

§39.5 5) Verdict

Passive limits are not viable on MCL under any condition this capture isolates — and unlike the index micros there is not even a frictionless mirage to argue over: the full shadow is −£87 frictionless before a penny of slippage. On the re-priced day the fills bled −£156 realistic (from −£68 ceiling, a −£87 adverse-selection tax) while passive additionally skipped +£116 of the bounces that would have paid. Win rate 27%; average loss double the average win. Every vol / rvol / slope / hour band with n ≥ 15 stays red; the one green slice is 7 trades of dead-quiet tape and the deeper-limit escape hatch is untestable because only the at-touch knob was captured. For MCL: keep it observe-only, or if you must trade crude do it aggressively — through-fills at least escape the no-fill asymmetry. Do not arm passive_chop on MCL.

The lab found nothing that pays net of costs — meaning the desk's current refusal here is correct. A real null is a result, not a failure.

§40 Passive Limit-Order Lab — MBT: a loser on paper, a worse loser in the tape

passive_chop rests a limit order and waits for price to come to it. Across the desk this week the paper (frictionless) ledger looked like a money machine and the realistic ledger bled — the culprit being adverse selection: a resting limit fills only when price trades through your level (you were wrong and it kept going), while the times price would have bounced back to pay you are no-fills. So you keep the losers and miss the winners. MBT is a special, blunt case. Here passive_chop did not even earn on paper — the frictionless ledger for MBT reads −$219 before a cent of friction. The realistic tape then deepens that to −$369 on the fills. There is no mirage to puncture: MBT passive is red frictionless, redder real, and every condition band I could slice was negative. Verdict: passive is not viable for MBT — adverse selection is fatal on top of an already-negative signal. Stay aggressive or don't trade it.

78 / 79
passive fills vs no-fills (MBT, this week)
−$155
frictionless P&L on the filled trades
−$369
REAL P&L on the same filled trades
−$214
the friction gap — adverse selection + spread

Scope: the two passive limit strategies that ran on MBT — passive_chop (280 signals) and passive_chop_calm (19) — of which 157 were re-priced trade-by-trade against the 250 ms quote tape in shadow_real.db (entry_model='passive'). Trading week UTC, from the recorded shadow session on this contract. Money is at the MBT ×0.1 multiplier (Micro Bitcoin). The frictionless run on all 299 passive/calm signals is −$331.9 — negative before friction is even applied.

§40.1 1. The gap: paper is already red, the tape is redder

Split MBT's re-priced passive record into what filled and what didn't, frictionless (ceiling_pnl) vs realistic (real_pnl):

BucketnFrictionless (ceiling)Realistic (real)Gap
Filled — you entered78−$155.3−$368.8−$213.5
No-fill — never entered79−$63.7—unreachable
Whole re-priced universe157−$219.0−$368.8—

Read it plainly. On most contracts the frictionless number is a green temptation that friction erases. On MBT there is no temptation: even if you could magically fill every one of the 157 signals at the bar price, passive_chop still loses −$219. Only 78 of the 157 filled (a 49.7% fill rate), and they filled because price ran through the level — re-priced on the real tape they lose −$368.8. Filled win rate is just 13 of 78 (16.7%). The friction cost per filled trade is $2.74, against an MBT quoted spread that averaged $20.9 in BTC price (median ~$15) which at the ×0.1 multiplier is ~$2.09/side — so the gap is almost exactly the spread you cross as price slices through your resting order.

The gap, drawn

Every bar points down. Red = frictionless P&L on the filled trades (already negative). Dark red = the same trades on the real tape. Grey = the no-fill signals' frictionless value (also net-negative — these were mostly losers you dodged, not winners you missed).

§40.2 2. Adverse selection, dissected

Every one of the 78 fills was a through-fill (knob_through=1 on all 78) — the limit sat at touch and only caught price when it drove clean through. Break the fills by how they ended:

ExitnWinsAvg frictionlessAvg REALSum REAL
STOP (traded through & kept going)350−$5.85−$7.42−$259.8
FLAT_CLOCK (stale-out)379+$0.41−$3.67−$135.8
RATCHET (reverted — the bounce paid)64+$5.71+$4.46+$26.8

The shape is the same adverse-selection asymmetry seen desk-wide, just with the reverts starved out. The 35 STOP fills — price drove through the limit and never came back — are 0-for-35 and cost −$260 real. The 37 FLAT_CLOCK fills sit at roughly break-even frictionless (+$0.41 avg) but friction alone tips them to −$3.67 (−$136 total). Only 6 fills reverted cleanly enough to ratchet a profit (+$27). There simply are not enough genuine reverts among the fills to pay for the through-fills. Frictionless the whole set is −$155; friction adds the final −$214.

The winners you never got — and the losers you dodged

Now the no-fills, split by their frictionless value. This is the crux of the survivorship trap: a no-fill is not automatically a missed winner.

No-fill typenFrictionless ceilingMeaning
Missed winner (price bounced away in your favour)28+$145.5unreachable edge
Dodged loser (price never reached, then reversed against)51−$209.2you were lucky to miss
All no-fills79−$63.7net-negative population

There is genuinely +$145.5 of unreachable edge sitting in the 28 clean bounces you never filled — the classic missed winners. But you cannot cherry-pick them: they come bundled with 51 dodged losers worth −$209.2, and the no-fill population as a whole is net −$63.7 frictionless. Filling more of the no-fills (a shallower/more aggressive limit) would drag in more losers than winners. The +$145.5 is real but structurally out of reach for a resting order at this level — capturing it means paying up front, which is the aggressive model, not passive.

Where the money is, drawn

Green up = the +$145.5 of bounces you missed (unreachable). Everything else points down: the losers you dodged, and the fills you actually took. The reachable buckets are all red.

§40.3 3. Fertile hunt: is there any condition where MBT passive pays?

I sliced the 78 filled trades every way the data allows — side, entry RVOL, ATR (absolute), |VWAP slope|, hour-of-day, and two-way combinations of them. The result is a clean null. Every single-condition band is negative, and every two-way combo with n≥15 is negative. Router regime is uniformly INDEX on MBT, so it partitions nothing.

Condition bandn (fills)Real P&LWin %
LONG36−$156.822%
SHORT42−$212.012%
RVOL < 1 (quiet)59−$275.817%
RVOL ≥ 1 (active)19−$93.016%
ATR < 2522−$107.09%
ATR 25–4037−$196.511%
ATR ≥ 40 (highest vol)19−$65.237%
|VWAP slope| < 1 (flat)29−$123.521%
06:00–12:00 UTC30−$164.213%
12:00–18:00 UTC (US session)34−$109.024%
Best-leaning 2-way: high-ATR × US session14−$25.243%
NULL

There is no fertile pocket for MBT passive. The least-bad slice — highest-vol fills (ATR≥40) taken in the US afternoon — is still −$25.2 over n=14, and it fails the n≥15 bar anyway. Nothing crosses into positive real P&L at a defensible sample size. This is not a threshold I can tune around; the signal is negative frictionless before adverse selection even bites.

§40.4 4. The knob: can resting deeper skip the toxic fills?

The honest answer is that the recorded shadow gives me nothing to A/B here: every MBT passive row was logged at a single knob_offset = 0.0 (limit at touch) and every fill was a through-fill (knob_through = 1, 78/78). There is no shallower/deeper offset variation in the data to compare. What I can reason from the fill/no-fill split: pushing the limit deeper (more passive) would convert some of today's 78 through-fills into no-fills — but the marginal filled trade is a loser and the no-fill population is itself net-negative (−$64), so a deeper limit helps only by trading less, not by uncovering an edge. A shallower limit does the opposite: it drags in more of the −$209 dodged-loser pile. Neither offset turns MBT passive positive; the only knob that improves real P&L on this contract is the off switch.

§40.5 5. Verdict

MBT passive is not a frictionless mirage — it is worse. On most contracts the paper number seduces and friction erases it; on MBT the paper number is already −$219, and the real tape drags the fills to −$369 (16.7% win rate, 0-for-35 on the STOP fills). Adverse selection is present and textbook — 100% through-fills, +$146 of clean bounces left unreachable in the no-fills — but it is piled on top of a signal that had no edge to begin with. Every condition band, every two-way combo at n≥15, is negative. There is no shadow candidate to promote. Do not run passive_chop on MBT. If MBT is to be traded at all it must be aggressive (cross the spread) or left alone; a resting limit here systematically banks the continuations and misses the reverts on a contract whose micro-reversion signal is already unprofitable.

One watch item, not a pocket: the highest-volatility US-afternoon fills (ATR≥40, 12:00–18:00 UTC) are the least-bad slice at 43% win and only −$25 over 14 fills. That is a place to look again once more MBT tape accrues — it is not, on this week's evidence, a place to rest a limit.

Every figure was computed from GAZBOT's own captured data for the trading week (Mon 29 Jun → Fri 3 Jul 2026): the trades ledger, realistic 250ms/5s fills, and tens of thousands of 5-second bars per contract. Sixteen forensic agents produced the dig; independent skeptics re-ran every claim worth £250+/week, and their verdict is stamped on each section. Plain-English by design.
